                try:
                    del batch
                except NameError:
                    pass
                if self.device.type == 'cuda':
                    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
                gc.collect()

                new_bs = max(1, current_bs // 2)
                if new_bs == current_bs:
                    raise   # already at 1, can't go lower — re-raise
                print(
                    f'\n  [OOM] CUDA OOM at batch_size={current_bs}. '
                    f'Retrying with batch_size={new_bs} ...'
                )
                # Re-split tiles_np to the smaller batch and retry
                # (caller must handle the rest of the original batch)
                tiles_np  = tiles_np[:new_bs]
                current_bs = new_bs

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public: full image inference
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def run_full_reference(self, img_rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
            """
            Full tiled inference on one large image.

            Args:
                img_rgb: (H, W, 3) uint8 RGB.

            Returns:
                (H, W) uint8 argmax class map.
            """
            H, W  = img_rgb.shape[:2]
            T     = self.tile_size
            S     = self.stride
            C     = self.num_classes

            # Compute safe batch size based on image dimensions
            bs = _safe_batch_size(H, W, T, self.requested_bs, self.vram_budget_gb)

            # Pad image so tiles cover the edges cleanly
            pad_h = (math.ceil((H - T) / S) * S + T) - H if H > T else T - H
            pad_w = (math.ceil((W - T) / S) * S + T) - W if W > T else T - W
            if pad_h > 0 or pad_w > 0:
                img_rgb = cv2.copyMakeBorder(
                    img_rgb, 0, pad_h, 0, pad_w,
                    cv2.BORDER_REFLECT_101,
                )
            Hp, Wp = img_rgb.shape[:2]

            # Tile coordinate grid
            ys     = list(range(0, Hp - T + 1, S))
            xs     = list(range(0, Wp - T + 1, S))
            coords = [(y, x) for y in ys for x in xs]
            print(f'  Grid: {len(xs)}×{len(ys)} = {len(coords)} tiles'
                  f'  |  batch_size={bs}'
                  f'  |  ~{math.ceil(len(coords)/bs)} forward passes')

            # CPU accumulators — never touch the GPU
            prob_acc   = torch.zeros(C, Hp, Wp, dtype=torch.float32)

            # Process all tiles in batches
            # OOM inside _forward_batch will halve bs and retry the SAME sub-batch.
            # We therefore use a while-loop with a pointer instead of a for-loop.
            ptr = 0
            with tqdm(total=len(coords), desc='  Tiles', unit='tile',
                      leave=False, ncols=80) as pbar:
                while ptr < len(coords):
                    batch_coords = coords[ptr: ptr + bs]
                    tiles_np     = [
                        build_9channel(img_rgb[y:y+T, x:x+T])
                        for y, x in batch_coords
                    ]

                    probs_cpu, bs_used = self._forward_batch(tiles_np, bs)
                    # _forward_batch may have reduced bs_used < len(batch_coords)
                    # if OOM occurred. In that case only bs_used tiles were processed.
                    if bs_used < len(batch_coords):
                        # Only advance by bs_used; remaining tiles re-processed next iter
                        batch_coords = batch_coords[:bs_used]
                        bs = bs_used   # use smaller size for remaining batches

                    # Splat probabilities into accumulator
                    for i, (y, x) in enumerate(batch_coords):
                        p = probs_cpu[i]                       # (C, T, T) float16
                        w = self.hann_cpu.float()                      # (T, T) float16
                        prob_acc[:, y:y+T, x:x+T]   += p * w.unsqueeze(0)

                    ptr += len(batch_coords)
                    pbar.update(len(batch_coords))

            # Argmax directly on accumulated weighted probabilities.
            # Positive per-pixel normalization factors do not change argmax.
            # This avoids allocating multiple full-size temporary tensors.
            class_map = prob_acc.argmax(dim=0).numpy().astype(np.uint8)
            return class_map[:H, :W]

    def run(self, img_rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
            """
            Full tiled inference on one large image.

            Args:
                img_rgb: (H, W, 3) uint8 RGB.

            Returns:
                (H, W) uint8 argmax class map.
            """
            H, W  = img_rgb.shape[:2]
            T     = self.tile_size
            S     = self.stride
            C     = self.num_classes
            assert 0 < S <= T

            # Compute safe batch size based on image dimensions
            bs = _safe_batch_size(H, W, T, self.requested_bs, self.vram_budget_gb)

            # Pad image so tiles cover the edges cleanly
            pad_h = (math.ceil((H - T) / S) * S + T) - H if H > T else T - H
            pad_w = (math.ceil((W - T) / S) * S + T) - W if W > T else T - W
            if pad_h > 0 or pad_w > 0:
                img_rgb = cv2.copyMakeBorder(
                    img_rgb, 0, pad_h, 0, pad_w,
                    cv2.BORDER_REFLECT_101,
                )
            Hp, Wp = img_rgb.shape[:2]

            # Tile coordinate grid
            ys     = list(range(0, Hp - T + 1, S))
            xs     = list(range(0, Wp - T + 1, S))
            coords = [(y, x) for y in ys for x in xs]
            print(f'  Grid: {len(xs)}×{len(ys)} = {len(coords)} tiles'
                  f'  |  batch_size={bs}'
                  f'  |  ~{math.ceil(len(coords)/bs)} forward passes')

            # CPU accumulators — never touch the GPU
            prob_acc = torch.zeros(
                C, T, Wp, dtype=torch.float32
            )

            class_map = np.empty((H, W), dtype=np.uint8)
            active_y = 0

            def finalize_rows(start, count):

                valid_count = min(count, H - start)

                if valid_count <= 0:
                    return

                for offset in range(0, valid_count, 64):

                    end = min(offset + 64, valid_count)

                    labels = prob_acc[
                        :, offset:end, :W
                    ].argmax(dim=0)

                    class_map[
                        start + offset:start + end, :
                    ] = labels.numpy().astype(np.uint8)

            print(
                f"  Rolling accumulator: "
                f"{prob_acc.numel() * 4 / 1e9:.3f} GB"
            )

            # Process all tiles in batches
            # OOM inside _forward_batch will halve bs and retry the SAME sub-batch.
            # We therefore use a while-loop with a pointer instead of a for-loop.
            ptr = 0
            with tqdm(total=len(coords), desc='  Tiles', unit='tile',
                      leave=False, ncols=80) as pbar:
                while ptr < len(coords):
                    batch_coords = coords[ptr: ptr + bs]
                    tiles_np     = [
                        build_9channel(img_rgb[y:y+T, x:x+T])
                        for y, x in batch_coords
                    ]

                    probs_cpu, bs_used = self._forward_batch(tiles_np, bs)
                    # _forward_batch may have reduced bs_used < len(batch_coords)
                    # if OOM occurred. In that case only bs_used tiles were processed.
                    if bs_used < len(batch_coords):
                        # Only advance by bs_used; remaining tiles re-processed next iter
                        batch_coords = batch_coords[:bs_used]
                        bs = bs_used   # use smaller size for remaining batches

                    # Splat probabilities into accumulator
                    for i, (y, x) in enumerate(batch_coords):
                        p = probs_cpu[i]                       # (C, T, T) float16
                        w = self.hann_cpu.float()                      # (T, T) float16
                        if y != active_y:

                            shift = y - active_y

                            assert 0 < shift <= T

                            finalize_rows(active_y, shift)

                            remaining = T - shift

                            if remaining > 0:
                                prob_acc[:, :remaining, :] = (
                                    prob_acc[:, shift:, :].clone()
                                )

                            prob_acc[:, remaining:, :].zero_()

                            active_y = y

                        prob_acc[:, :, x:x+T] += (
                            p * w.unsqueeze(0)
                        )

                    ptr += len(batch_coords)
                    pbar.update(len(batch_coords))

            # Argmax directly on accumulated weighted probabilities.
            # Positive per-pixel normalization factors do not change argmax.
            # This avoids allocating multiple full-size temporary tensors.
            finalize_rows(active_y, T)

            del prob_acc

            return class_map

SOURCE FILE: src/inference/tile_v11_before_fp32.py
================================================================================

class TileStitcher:
    """
    Production-grade tiled inference over large orthomosaic images.

    All GPU memory is released after every batch.
    Automatically retries with smaller batch on CUDA OOM.
    All accumulator tensors live on CPU to avoid VRAM pressure.

    Args:
        model:         Trained SegFormerB2 on `device`, already in eval mode.
        device:        torch.device
        tile_size:     Tile size in pixels (default 1024).
        stride:        Stride between tile origins (default 768 = 256px overlap).
        batch_size:    Requested tiles per GPU batch. Automatically reduced
                       based on image size and VRAM budget.
        num_classes:   Number of segmentation classes.
        vram_budget_gb: VRAM budget for one forward pass (default 12 GB).
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        model: torch.nn.Module,
        device: torch.device,
        tile_size: int = 1024,
        stride: int = 768,
        batch_size: int = 8,
        num_classes: int = 7,
        vram_budget_gb: float = 12.0,
    ) -> None:
        self.model          = model
        self.device         = device
        self.tile_size      = tile_size
        self.stride         = stride
        self.requested_bs   = batch_size
        self.num_classes    = num_classes
        self.vram_budget_gb = vram_budget_gb
        # Keep Hann window on CPU — moved to GPU per-batch only
        self.hann_cpu       = _hann2d(tile_size)   # (T, T) float16 on CPU
        model.eval()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Internal: forward one batch with OOM retry
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _forward_batch(
        self,
        tiles_np: list[np.ndarray],
        current_bs: int,
    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, int]:
        """
        Run one forward pass. On CUDA OOM, halve the batch and retry.

        Returns:
            (probs_cpu, effective_bs_used)
            probs_cpu: (B, C, T, T) float16 on CPU
        """
        while True:
            try:
                batch = torch.stack(
                    [torch.from_numpy(t) for t in tiles_np]
                ).to(self.device)                          # (B, 9, T, T) float32

                with torch.no_grad():
                    logits    = self.model(batch)          # (B, C, T, T)
                    logits[:, 6] -= 1.20  # V11 drought calibration
                    logits[:, 5] -= 0.40  # V11 dry-weed calibration
                    probs_cpu = F.softmax(logits, dim=1).cpu().half()

                # Explicit GPU cleanup — critical for sustained batch runs
                del batch, logits
                if self.device.type == 'cuda':
                    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
                gc.collect()

                return probs_cpu, current_bs

            except torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError:
                # Clean up whatever partial allocations were made
                try:
                    del batch
                except NameError:
                    pass
                if self.device.type == 'cuda':
                    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
                gc.collect()

                new_bs = max(1, current_bs // 2)
                if new_bs == current_bs:
                    raise   # already at 1, can't go lower — re-raise
                print(
                    f'\n  [OOM] CUDA OOM at batch_size={current_bs}. '
                    f'Retrying with batch_size={new_bs} ...'
                )
                # Re-split tiles_np to the smaller batch and retry
                # (caller must handle the rest of the original batch)
                tiles_np  = tiles_np[:new_bs]
                current_bs = new_bs

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public: full image inference
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def run(self, img_rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """
        Full tiled inference on one large image.

        Args:
            img_rgb: (H, W, 3) uint8 RGB.

        Returns:
            (H, W) uint8 argmax class map.
        """
        H, W  = img_rgb.shape[:2]
        T     = self.tile_size
        S     = self.stride
        C     = self.num_classes

        # Compute safe batch size based on image dimensions
        bs = _safe_batch_size(H, W, T, self.requested_bs, self.vram_budget_gb)

        # Pad image so tiles cover the edges cleanly
        pad_h = (math.ceil((H - T) / S) * S + T) - H if H > T else T - H
        pad_w = (math.ceil((W - T) / S) * S + T) - W if W > T else T - W
        if pad_h > 0 or pad_w > 0:
            img_rgb = cv2.copyMakeBorder(
                img_rgb, 0, pad_h, 0, pad_w,
                cv2.BORDER_REFLECT_101,
            )
        Hp, Wp = img_rgb.shape[:2]

        # Tile coordinate grid
        ys     = list(range(0, Hp - T + 1, S))
        xs     = list(range(0, Wp - T + 1, S))
        coords = [(y, x) for y in ys for x in xs]
        print(f'  Grid: {len(xs)}×{len(ys)} = {len(coords)} tiles'
              f'  |  batch_size={bs}'
              f'  |  ~{math.ceil(len(coords)/bs)} forward passes')

        # CPU accumulators — never touch the GPU
        prob_acc   = torch.zeros(C, Hp, Wp, dtype=torch.float16)
        weight_acc = torch.zeros(1, Hp, Wp, dtype=torch.float16)

        # Process all tiles in batches
        # OOM inside _forward_batch will halve bs and retry the SAME sub-batch.
        # We therefore use a while-loop with a pointer instead of a for-loop.
        ptr = 0
        with tqdm(total=len(coords), desc='  Tiles', unit='tile',
                  leave=False, ncols=80) as pbar:
            while ptr < len(coords):
                batch_coords = coords[ptr: ptr + bs]
                tiles_np     = [
                    build_9channel(img_rgb[y:y+T, x:x+T])
                    for y, x in batch_coords
                ]

                probs_cpu, bs_used = self._forward_batch(tiles_np, bs)
                # _forward_batch may have reduced bs_used < len(batch_coords)
                # if OOM occurred. In that case only bs_used tiles were processed.
                if bs_used < len(batch_coords):
                    # Only advance by bs_used; remaining tiles re-processed next iter
                    batch_coords = batch_coords[:bs_used]
                    bs = bs_used   # use smaller size for remaining batches

                # Splat probabilities into accumulator
                for i, (y, x) in enumerate(batch_coords):
                    p = probs_cpu[i]                       # (C, T, T) float16
                    w = self.hann_cpu                      # (T, T) float16
                    prob_acc[:, y:y+T, x:x+T]   += p * w.unsqueeze(0)
                    weight_acc[0, y:y+T, x:x+T] += w

                ptr += len(batch_coords)
                pbar.update(len(batch_coords))

        # Argmax directly on accumulated weighted probabilities.
        # Positive per-pixel normalization factors do not change argmax.
        # This avoids allocating multiple full-size temporary tensors.
        class_map = prob_acc.argmax(dim=0).numpy().astype(np.uint8)
        return class_map[:H, :W]




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/inference/tile_v11_before_weight_removal.py
================================================================================

"""
Tile-and-stitch inference for large orthomosaic images.

Production-hardened for very large remote sensing imagery (25k+ pixels).

Fixes applied
-------------
1. All accumulators (prob_acc, weight_acc) live on CPU float16.
   Nothing is accumulated on the GPU — only one batch at a time sits there.

2. Dynamic safe batch size: computed from image pixel count to avoid OOM
   before the first forward pass. Can be overridden by the caller.

3. OOM retry loop: if a batch still OOMs (e.g. due to fragmentation), the
   batch is automatically re-tried with half the size, down to minimum 1.

4. Explicit GPU cleanup after every batch: del + empty_cache + gc.collect.
   Prevents VRAM fragmentation across the hundreds of batches in a large image.

5. No model-side changes required — fixes are purely in data movement.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import gc
import math

import cv2
import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from tqdm import tqdm

from src.preprocessing.tensor_builder import build_9channel


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Dynamic safe batch size
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _safe_batch_size(
    H: int,
    W: int,
    tile_size: int,
    requested: int,
    vram_budget_gb: float = 12.0,
) -> int:
    """
    Compute a safe batch size that is unlikely to OOM on the current GPU.

    Heuristic based on measured GPU usage for SegFormer-B2 at tile_size=1024:
        ~0.22 GB per tile in a batch (input + activations + logits)
    This is conservative — actual usage depends on batch size due to
    batched attention patterns in SegFormer.

    Args:
        H, W:          Image dimensions in pixels.
        tile_size:     Inference tile size.
        requested:     Caller-requested batch size.
        vram_budget_gb: How many GB to budget for one forward pass.
                        Default 12 GB leaves headroom for the model weights
                        (~0.5 GB) on a 70 GB H200.
    """
    pixels_per_tile = tile_size * tile_size
    # Measured ~0.22 GB per tile for SegFormer-B2 at 1024×1024
    # Measured SegFormer-B2 peak VRAM per tile in a batch:
    # ~0.75 GB at 1024×1024 — accounts for input, activations, attention maps,
    # gradients (even with no_grad, PyTorch keeps intermediate tensors).
    gb_per_tile = 9 * pixels_per_tile * 4 / 1e9 * 20.0  # 20× overhead factor

    safe = max(1, int(vram_budget_gb / gb_per_tile))
    result = min(requested, safe)

    total_tiles = math.ceil(H / tile_size) * math.ceil(W / tile_size)
    if result < requested:
        print(
            f'  [tile] Image {W}×{H} ({total_tiles} tiles): '
            f'reducing batch {requested}→{result} to stay within '
            f'{vram_budget_gb:.0f} GB VRAM budget.'
        )
    return result


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Hann window
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _hann2d(size: int) -> torch.Tensor:
    """2D Hann window as CPU float16. Clipped to min 0.01 so corners contribute."""
    h = torch.hann_window(size, periodic=False)
    h = h.clamp(min=0.1)
    return torch.outer(h, h).half()   # (size, size) float16


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# TileStitcher
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class TileStitcher:
    """
    Production-grade tiled inference over large orthomosaic images.

    All GPU memory is released after every batch.
    Automatically retries with smaller batch on CUDA OOM.
    All accumulator tensors live on CPU to avoid VRAM pressure.

    Args:
        model:         Trained SegFormerB2 on `device`, already in eval mode.
        device:        torch.device
        tile_size:     Tile size in pixels (default 1024).
        stride:        Stride between tile origins (default 768 = 256px overlap).
        batch_size:    Requested tiles per GPU batch. Automatically reduced
                       based on image size and VRAM budget.
        num_classes:   Number of segmentation classes.
        vram_budget_gb: VRAM budget for one forward pass (default 12 GB).
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        model: torch.nn.Module,
        device: torch.device,
        tile_size: int = 1024,
        stride: int = 768,
        batch_size: int = 8,
        num_classes: int = 7,
        vram_budget_gb: float = 12.0,
    ) -> None:
        self.model          = model
        self.device         = device
        self.tile_size      = tile_size
        self.stride         = stride
        self.requested_bs   = batch_size
        self.num_classes    = num_classes
        self.vram_budget_gb = vram_budget_gb
        # Keep Hann window on CPU — moved to GPU per-batch only
        self.hann_cpu       = _hann2d(tile_size)   # (T, T) float16 on CPU
        model.eval()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Internal: forward one batch with OOM retry
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _forward_batch(
        self,
        tiles_np: list[np.ndarray],
        current_bs: int,
    ) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, int]:
        """
        Run one forward pass. On CUDA OOM, halve the batch and retry.

        Returns:
            (probs_cpu, effective_bs_used)
            probs_cpu: (B, C, T, T) float16 on CPU
        """
        while True:
            try:
                batch = torch.stack(
                    [torch.from_numpy(t) for t in tiles_np]
                ).to(self.device)                          # (B, 9, T, T) float32

                with torch.no_grad():
                    logits    = self.model(batch)          # (B, C, T, T)
                    logits[:, 6] -= 1.20  # V11 drought calibration
                    logits[:, 5] -= 0.40  # V11 dry-weed calibration
                    probs_cpu = F.softmax(logits, dim=1).cpu().float()

                # Explicit GPU cleanup — critical for sustained batch runs
                del batch, logits
                if self.device.type == 'cuda':
                    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
                gc.collect()

                return probs_cpu, current_bs

            except torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError:
                # Clean up whatever partial allocations were made
                try:
                    del batch
                except NameError:
                    pass
                if self.device.type == 'cuda':
                    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
                gc.collect()

                new_bs = max(1, current_bs // 2)
                if new_bs == current_bs:
                    raise   # already at 1, can't go lower — re-raise
                print(
                    f'\n  [OOM] CUDA OOM at batch_size={current_bs}. '
                    f'Retrying with batch_size={new_bs} ...'
                )
                # Re-split tiles_np to the smaller batch and retry
                # (caller must handle the rest of the original batch)
                tiles_np  = tiles_np[:new_bs]
                current_bs = new_bs

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public: full image inference
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def run(self, img_rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """
        Full tiled inference on one large image.

        Args:
            img_rgb: (H, W, 3) uint8 RGB.

        Returns:
            (H, W) uint8 argmax class map.
        """
        H, W  = img_rgb.shape[:2]
        T     = self.tile_size
        S     = self.stride
        C     = self.num_classes

        # Compute safe batch size based on image dimensions
        bs = _safe_batch_size(H, W, T, self.requested_bs, self.vram_budget_gb)

        # Pad image so tiles cover the edges cleanly
        pad_h = (math.ceil((H - T) / S) * S + T) - H if H > T else T - H
        pad_w = (math.ceil((W - T) / S) * S + T) - W if W > T else T - W
        if pad_h > 0 or pad_w > 0:
            img_rgb = cv2.copyMakeBorder(
                img_rgb, 0, pad_h, 0, pad_w,
                cv2.BORDER_REFLECT_101,
            )
        Hp, Wp = img_rgb.shape[:2]

        # Tile coordinate grid
        ys     = list(range(0, Hp - T + 1, S))
        xs     = list(range(0, Wp - T + 1, S))
        coords = [(y, x) for y in ys for x in xs]
        print(f'  Grid: {len(xs)}×{len(ys)} = {len(coords)} tiles'
              f'  |  batch_size={bs}'
              f'  |  ~{math.ceil(len(coords)/bs)} forward passes')

        # CPU accumulators — never touch the GPU
        prob_acc   = torch.zeros(C, Hp, Wp, dtype=torch.float32)
        weight_acc = torch.zeros(1, Hp, Wp, dtype=torch.float32)

        # Process all tiles in batches
        # OOM inside _forward_batch will halve bs and retry the SAME sub-batch.
        # We therefore use a while-loop with a pointer instead of a for-loop.
        ptr = 0
        with tqdm(total=len(coords), desc='  Tiles', unit='tile',
                  leave=False, ncols=80) as pbar:
            while ptr < len(coords):
                batch_coords = coords[ptr: ptr + bs]
                tiles_np     = [
                    build_9channel(img_rgb[y:y+T, x:x+T])
                    for y, x in batch_coords
                ]

                probs_cpu, bs_used = self._forward_batch(tiles_np, bs)
                # _forward_batch may have reduced bs_used < len(batch_coords)
                # if OOM occurred. In that case only bs_used tiles were processed.
                if bs_used < len(batch_coords):
                    # Only advance by bs_used; remaining tiles re-processed next iter
                    batch_coords = batch_coords[:bs_used]
                    bs = bs_used   # use smaller size for remaining batches

                # Splat probabilities into accumulator
                for i, (y, x) in enumerate(batch_coords):
                    p = probs_cpu[i]                       # (C, T, T) float16
                    w = self.hann_cpu.float()                      # (T, T) float16
                    prob_acc[:, y:y+T, x:x+T]   += p * w.unsqueeze(0)
                    weight_acc[0, y:y+T, x:x+T] += w

                ptr += len(batch_coords)
                pbar.update(len(batch_coords))

        # Argmax directly on accumulated weighted probabilities.
        # Positive per-pixel normalization factors do not change argmax.
        # This avoids allocating multiple full-size temporary tensors.
        class_map = prob_acc.argmax(dim=0).numpy().astype(np.uint8)
        return class_map[:H, :W]

================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/preprocessing/gsd.py
================================================================================

"""
Ground Sample Distance (GSD) calculation.

GSD = pixel_size × altitude / focal_length

Verified against the user's Metashape report:
    pixel_size = 1.31 μm, focal = 6.72 mm, altitude = 13.8 m
    → GSD = 1.31e-3 × 13800 / 6.72 = 2.690 mm/pix  ✓ (report says 2.69 mm/pix)

This module is the single source of truth for converting pixels to m² in reports.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CameraIntrinsics:
    """Camera intrinsics. Defaults are from the user's Metashape report (DJI FC8482)."""
    pixel_size_um: float = 1.31
    focal_length_mm: float = 6.72
    native_width_px: int = 8064
    native_height_px: int = 4536


def gsd_mm_per_pixel(altitude_m: float, cam: CameraIntrinsics | None = None) -> float:
    """
    Compute Ground Sample Distance in mm/pixel for a given flight altitude.

    Args:
        altitude_m: Flight altitude above ground level, in meters.
        cam: Camera intrinsics. Defaults to the FC8482 from the report.

    Returns:
        GSD in mm/pixel.
    """
    if cam is None:
        cam = CameraIntrinsics()
    if altitude_m <= 0:
        raise ValueError(f"altitude_m must be positive; got {altitude_m}")
    # pixel_size [mm] × altitude [mm] / focal_length [mm]  →  [mm/pix]
    pixel_size_mm = cam.pixel_size_um * 1e-3
    altitude_mm = altitude_m * 1e3
    return pixel_size_mm * altitude_mm / cam.focal_length_mm


def pixel_area_m2(altitude_m: float, cam: CameraIntrinsics | None = None) -> float:
    """Area covered by one pixel on the ground, in m²."""
    gsd_mm = gsd_mm_per_pixel(altitude_m, cam)
    gsd_m = gsd_mm * 1e-3
    return gsd_m * gsd_m


def pixels_to_m2(num_pixels: int, altitude_m: float, cam: CameraIntrinsics | None = None) -> float:
    """Convert a pixel count to ground area in m²."""
    return num_pixels * pixel_area_m2(altitude_m, cam)


def pixels_to_hectares(num_pixels: int, altitude_m: float, cam: CameraIntrinsics | None = None) -> float:
    """Convert a pixel count to ground area in hectares."""
    return pixels_to_m2(num_pixels, altitude_m, cam) / 10_000.0


# Sanity-check on import (catches regressions)
def _self_test() -> None:
    cam = CameraIntrinsics()
    # The Metashape report says GSD = 2.69 mm/pix at altitude 13.8m
    gsd = gsd_mm_per_pixel(13.8, cam)
    assert abs(gsd - 2.69) < 0.01, f"GSD self-test failed: got {gsd:.3f}, expected ~2.69"


_self_test()




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/preprocessing/indices.py
================================================================================

"""
Vegetation indices computed from RGB.

All functions accept a float32 array shaped (..., 3) with values in [0, 1]
(R, G, B in the last axis) and return a same-shape-minus-last-axis float32 array.

Indices implemented:
    ExG   = 2G - R - B                       Excess Green
    ExGR  = ExG - (1.4 R - G)                Excess Green minus Excess Red
    CIVE  = 0.441 R - 0.811 G + 0.385 B + c  Color Index of Vegetation Extraction
    GLI   = (2G - R - B) / (2G + R + B)      Green Leaf Index
    NGRDI = (G - R) / (G + R)                Normalized Green-Red Diff Index

All are normalized to roughly [-1, 1] for stable neural network input.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np

_EPS = 1e-6


def _split(rgb: np.ndarray) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
    """Split (..., 3) array into r, g, b each (...)."""
    return rgb[..., 0], rgb[..., 1], rgb[..., 2]


def exg(rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Excess Green. Range roughly [-2, 2], typically [-0.5, 0.5] for natural scenes."""
    r, g, b = _split(rgb)
    val = 2.0 * g - r - b
    # Normalize to roughly [-1, 1]: divide by 2 (theoretical max)
    return (val / 2.0).astype(np.float32)


def exgr(rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """ExG - ExR. Better at suppressing soil response than plain ExG."""
    r, g, b = _split(rgb)
    val = (2.0 * g - r - b) - (1.4 * r - g)
    return (val / 3.0).astype(np.float32)


def cive(rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Color Index of Vegetation Extraction. Lower = more vegetation."""
    r, g, b = _split(rgb)
    # Original CIVE has an offset of 18.78745 calibrated for uint8 input;
    # for [0,1] input that offset would dwarf the signal, so we drop it and rescale.
    val = 0.441 * r - 0.811 * g + 0.385 * b
    # Invert so higher = more vegetation, and scale to [-1, 1]
    return (-val / 0.811).astype(np.float32)


def gli(rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Green Leaf Index. Inherently in [-1, 1]."""
    r, g, b = _split(rgb)
    num = 2.0 * g - r - b
    den = 2.0 * g + r + b + _EPS
    return np.clip(num / den, -1.0, 1.0).astype(np.float32)


def ngrdi(rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Normalized Green-Red Difference Index. Inherently in [-1, 1]."""
    r, g, _ = _split(rgb)
    num = g - r
    den = g + r + _EPS
    return np.clip(num / den, -1.0, 1.0).astype(np.float32)


# All indices in the order we stack them as input channels (4..8)
INDEX_FUNCS = (exg, exgr, cive, gli, ngrdi)
INDEX_NAMES = ("ExG", "ExGR", "CIVE", "GLI", "NGRDI")


def compute_all_indices(rgb_float01: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Compute all 5 vegetation indices and return them stacked.

    Args:
        rgb_float01: (H, W, 3) float32 with values in [0, 1]

    Returns:
        (5, H, W) float32, channels in order: ExG, ExGR, CIVE, GLI, NGRDI
    """
    if rgb_float01.dtype != np.float32:
        rgb_float01 = rgb_float01.astype(np.float32)
    if rgb_float01.max() > 1.0 + _EPS:
        raise ValueError(
            f"compute_all_indices expects values in [0,1]; got max={rgb_float01.max()}"
        )
    out = np.stack([f(rgb_float01) for f in INDEX_FUNCS], axis=0)
    return out




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/preprocessing/masks.py
================================================================================

"""
Auxiliary binary masks for preprocessing.

Three masks computed per image:
    valid_field_mask: True where the image is actual field data (not background/no-data)
    glare_mask:       True where specular highlights/sun glare dominate
    shadow_mask:      True where cast shadows are deep enough to suppress useful signal

All functions take a uint8 (H, W, 3) RGB array and return a (H, W) bool array.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import cv2
import numpy as np


def _rgb_to_hsv_float(rgb_uint8: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Convert uint8 RGB to float HSV in [0, 1] for H, S, V."""
    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(rgb_uint8, cv2.COLOR_RGB2HSV).astype(np.float32)
    hsv[..., 0] /= 179.0  # OpenCV H is [0, 179]
    hsv[..., 1] /= 255.0
    hsv[..., 2] /= 255.0
    return hsv


def glare_mask(
    rgb_uint8: np.ndarray,
    v_thresh: float = 0.95,
    s_thresh: float = 0.10,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Detect specular highlights / sun glare.

    A pixel is glare iff:
        Value (brightness) > v_thresh AND Saturation < s_thresh
    i.e., extremely bright and nearly desaturated.

    This catches sun glare on leaves (which would otherwise look like dry/drought) and
    sun glare on soil (which would otherwise look like cotton balls).
    """
    hsv = _rgb_to_hsv_float(rgb_uint8)
    return (hsv[..., 2] > v_thresh) & (hsv[..., 1] < s_thresh)


def shadow_mask(
    rgb_uint8: np.ndarray,
    v_thresh: float = 0.15,
    s_thresh: float = 0.20,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Detect deep cast shadows.

    A pixel is shadow iff Value < v_thresh AND Saturation < s_thresh.
    Deep shadows under canopy can otherwise look like dark/dead vegetation.
    """
    hsv = _rgb_to_hsv_float(rgb_uint8)
    return (hsv[..., 2] < v_thresh) & (hsv[..., 1] < s_thresh)


def valid_field_mask(
    rgb_uint8: np.ndarray,
    dark_threshold: int = 10,
    saturation_threshold: int = 15,
    min_border_component_area: int = 500,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Compute a binary mask: True = valid field data, False = background/no-data.

    Detects TWO types of Metashape no-data padding:
        1. Dark/black padding  — max channel <= dark_threshold (original behaviour)
        2. White/near-white    — max channel >= 248 AND saturation <= 8
           Metashape fills outside-coverage areas with white (255,255,255) by default.

    Threshold for white padding (max>=248, sat<=8) is safe:
        cotton_ball pixels: saturation ~15  → NOT masked
        bright soil:        saturation ~60  → NOT masked
        glare on leaves:    saturation ~15  → NOT masked

    Uses cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats (~50x faster than scipy.ndimage.label).
    """
    h, w = rgb_uint8.shape[:2]

    # Candidate background: dark pixels OR white/near-white padding
    max_channel = rgb_uint8.max(axis=2)
    min_channel = rgb_uint8.min(axis=2)
    saturation  = max_channel.astype(np.int16) - min_channel.astype(np.int16)

    dark_bg  = (max_channel <= dark_threshold) & (saturation <= saturation_threshold)
    white_bg = (max_channel >= 248) & (saturation <= 8)   # ← NEW: white padding
    candidate_bg = (dark_bg | white_bg).astype(np.uint8)

    if not candidate_bg.any():
        return np.ones((h, w), dtype=bool)

    # cv2 connected components (int32, SIMD-accelerated)
    n_labels, labels, stats, _ = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(
        candidate_bg, connectivity=8
    )
    if n_labels <= 1:
        return np.ones((h, w), dtype=bool)

    # Find labels touching any image border
    border_labels = set()
    border_labels.update(labels[0,  :].tolist())
    border_labels.update(labels[-1, :].tolist())
    border_labels.update(labels[:,  0].tolist())
    border_labels.update(labels[:, -1].tolist())
    border_labels.discard(0)

    if not border_labels:
        return np.ones((h, w), dtype=bool)

    # Keep only border-touching components above minimum size
    keep = [
        lbl for lbl in border_labels
        if int(stats[lbl, cv2.CC_STAT_AREA]) >= min_border_component_area
    ]
    if not keep:
        return np.ones((h, w), dtype=bool)

    # Build background mask and dilate slightly to catch soft edges
    bg = np.isin(labels, keep).astype(np.uint8)
    del labels, stats   # free immediately (2.5 GB for 25k×25k)

    kernel = cv2.getStructuringElement(cv2.MORPH_RECT, (5, 5))
    bg = cv2.dilate(bg, kernel, iterations=2)
    return ~bg.astype(bool)


def compute_aux_masks(
    rgb_uint8: np.ndarray,
    *,
    glare_v: float = 0.95,
    glare_s: float = 0.10,
    shadow_v: float = 0.15,
    shadow_s: float = 0.20,
    field_dark: int = 10,
    field_sat: int = 15,
    field_min_area: int = 500,
) -> dict[str, np.ndarray]:
    """One-shot computation of all auxiliary masks. Returns a dict of bool arrays."""
    return {
        "valid_field": valid_field_mask(rgb_uint8, field_dark, field_sat, field_min_area),
        "glare": glare_mask(rgb_uint8, glare_v, glare_s),
        "shadow": shadow_mask(rgb_uint8, shadow_v, shadow_s),
    }




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/preprocessing/tensor_builder.py
================================================================================

"""
Build the multi-channel input tensor used across the whole pipeline.

Three flavors:
    build_9channel  — RGB + 5 vegetation indices + HSV-V. Used by patch classifier and
                      segmentation model.
    build_12channel — adds local-std, GLCM-contrast, FFT-row-score. Used by pseudo-
                      labeling and orthomosaic inference where the bigger context lets
                      the structural cues become meaningful. (Computed on demand;
                      relatively expensive.)
    build_rgb_norm  — just the ImageNet-normalized RGB, for cases where indices are
                      computed separately.

All builders return float32 tensors shaped (C, H, W).
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import cv2
import numpy as np

from .indices import compute_all_indices

# ImageNet stats for RGB normalization
_IMAGENET_MEAN = np.array([0.485, 0.456, 0.406], dtype=np.float32)
_IMAGENET_STD = np.array([0.229, 0.224, 0.225], dtype=np.float32)


def _normalize_rgb(rgb_uint8: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """uint8 (H,W,3) → ImageNet-normalized float32 (3,H,W)."""
    rgb = rgb_uint8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0
    rgb = (rgb - _IMAGENET_MEAN) / _IMAGENET_STD
    return rgb.transpose(2, 0, 1).astype(np.float32)


def _rgb_to_v_channel(rgb_uint8: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """Extract HSV V channel only, in [-1, 1]."""
    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(rgb_uint8, cv2.COLOR_RGB2HSV)
    v = hsv[..., 2].astype(np.float32) / 255.0
    # Center around 0 for symmetry with the indices
    return (v * 2.0 - 1.0).astype(np.float32)


def build_9channel(rgb_uint8: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Build the 9-channel input tensor: RGB(3) + indices(5) + V(1).

    Args:
        rgb_uint8: (H, W, 3) uint8 RGB image.

    Returns:
        (9, H, W) float32 tensor.
    """
    if rgb_uint8.ndim != 3 or rgb_uint8.shape[2] != 3:
        raise ValueError(f"Expected (H,W,3) RGB; got shape {rgb_uint8.shape}")
    if rgb_uint8.dtype != np.uint8:
        raise ValueError(f"Expected uint8; got {rgb_uint8.dtype}")

    rgb_norm = _normalize_rgb(rgb_uint8)            # (3, H, W)
    rgb_float01 = rgb_uint8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0
    indices = compute_all_indices(rgb_float01)      # (5, H, W)
    v = _rgb_to_v_channel(rgb_uint8)                # (H, W)

    return np.concatenate([rgb_norm, indices, v[None]], axis=0).astype(np.float32)


# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Structural channels (computed only on demand)
# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def _local_std(gray_uint8: np.ndarray, ksize: int = 5) -> np.ndarray:
    """Local std of luminance over a ksize×ksize window. Boll-detection proxy."""
    g = gray_uint8.astype(np.float32)
    mean = cv2.boxFilter(g, ddepth=-1, ksize=(ksize, ksize), normalize=True)
    mean_sq = cv2.boxFilter(g * g, ddepth=-1, ksize=(ksize, ksize), normalize=True)
    var = np.maximum(mean_sq - mean * mean, 0.0)
    std = np.sqrt(var)
    # Scale to roughly [-1, 1] — typical std for natural scenes is 10-40 in [0,255]
    return (std / 40.0 * 2.0 - 1.0).astype(np.float32)


def _glcm_contrast_blocked(gray_uint8: np.ndarray, block: int = 32) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Approximate GLCM contrast computed on blocks for speed, upsampled back.
    Uses a fast per-block variance proxy (Laplacian variance), which is highly
    correlated with GLCM contrast for our purposes.
    """
    h, w = gray_uint8.shape
    lap = cv2.Laplacian(gray_uint8.astype(np.float32), ddepth=cv2.CV_32F, ksize=3)
    lap_sq = lap * lap
    # Downsample by mean-pooling
    h_blocks = h // block
    w_blocks = w // block
    if h_blocks == 0 or w_blocks == 0:
        return np.zeros_like(gray_uint8, dtype=np.float32)
    trimmed = lap_sq[: h_blocks * block, : w_blocks * block]
    pooled = trimmed.reshape(h_blocks, block, w_blocks, block).mean(axis=(1, 3))
    # Upsample to original size (nearest is fine — these are coarse cues)
    upsampled = cv2.resize(pooled, (w, h), interpolation=cv2.INTER_LINEAR)
    # Scale to [-1, 1]; typical values for natural scenes saturate around 200
    return np.clip(upsampled / 200.0 * 2.0 - 1.0, -1.0, 1.0).astype(np.float32)


def _fft_row_periodicity_blocked(gray_uint8: np.ndarray, block: int = 256) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Score row-crop periodicity per block using FFT.
    Returns a (H, W) map; higher = stronger periodic row structure.
    """
    h, w = gray_uint8.shape
    score = np.zeros((h, w), dtype=np.float32)
    g = gray_uint8.astype(np.float32) - gray_uint8.mean()

    for by in range(0, h - block + 1, block):
        for bx in range(0, w - block + 1, block):
            patch = g[by:by + block, bx:bx + block]
            # 2D FFT and power spectrum
            f = np.fft.fft2(patch)
            ps = np.abs(f) ** 2
            ps[0, 0] = 0  # remove DC
            # Sum of top-K bins divided by total energy → periodicity score
            total = ps.sum()
            if total <= 0:
                continue
            top_k = np.partition(ps.flatten(), -10)[-10:].sum()
            s = top_k / total
            # Typical range ~0.02-0.2; map to roughly [-1, 1]
            score[by:by + block, bx:bx + block] = np.clip(s * 10.0 - 1.0, -1.0, 1.0)

    return score


def build_12channel(rgb_uint8: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Build the 12-channel input tensor: 9-channel + local-std + GLCM-contrast + FFT-row.

    Used during pseudo-labeling and orthomosaic inference, where the larger spatial
    context makes the structural cues informative.
    """
    base = build_9channel(rgb_uint8)
    gray = cv2.cvtColor(rgb_uint8, cv2.COLOR_RGB2GRAY)
    s = _local_std(gray)
    c = _glcm_contrast_blocked(gray)
    f = _fft_row_periodicity_blocked(gray)
    structural = np.stack([s, c, f], axis=0).astype(np.float32)
    return np.concatenate([base, structural], axis=0).astype(np.float32)


CHANNEL_NAMES_9 = ("R", "G", "B", "ExG", "ExGR", "CIVE", "GLI", "NGRDI", "V")
CHANNEL_NAMES_12 = CHANNEL_NAMES_9 + ("LocalStd", "GLCM", "FFTrow")




================================================================================

"""Pseudo-labeling of unlabeled UAV captures using the patch classifier."""
from .label import label
from .postprocess import apply_crf, postprocess_probmap
from .sliding_window import SlidingWindowInference

    "label",
    "apply_crf",

    "SlidingWindowInference",
