================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/pseudo_label/label.py
================================================================================

"""
Pseudo-label the unlabeled UAV images using the trained patch classifier.

For each image in data/unlabeled/:
    1. Two-pass sliding-window inference (256×256 + 128×128).
    2. Dense CRF refinement on RGB pairwise terms.
    3. Structural-cue overrides (glare, FFT row score, ExG rules).
    4. Confidence thresholding → IGNORE_INDEX (255) for uncertain pixels.
    5. Save PNG mask (palettized) + confidence map.

Outputs (under runs/pseudo_labels/):
    masks/          one PNG per image — uint8, pixel value = class index
                    (255 = ignore)
    overlays/       semi-transparent overlay on original image (for inspection)
    confidence/     float32 confidence map saved as 8-bit PNG (0=0.0, 255=1.0)
    manifest.yaml   per-image stats: path, ignore_fraction, class_fractions

Usage:
    python -m src.pseudo_label.label --config configs/config.yaml
    python -m src.pseudo_label.label --config configs/config.yaml --limit 5  # test on 5 images
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import time
from pathlib import Path

import cv2
import numpy as np
import torch
import yaml
from tqdm import tqdm

from src.classifier.model import MultiscalePatchClassifier
from src.pseudo_label.postprocess import postprocess_probmap
from src.pseudo_label.sliding_window import SlidingWindowInference
from src.utils.constants import (
    CLASS_NAMES,
    IGNORE_INDEX,
    NUM_CLASSES,
    PALETTE,
)
from src.pseudo_label.viz import (
    overlay_with_legend,
    add_stat_bar,
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def load_image(path: Path) -> np.ndarray | None:
    """Load a JPG/PNG as uint8 RGB. Returns None on failure."""
    img = cv2.imread(str(path), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
    if img is None:
        return None
    return cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)


def save_mask_png(mask: np.ndarray, path: Path) -> None:
    """Save a uint8 class-index mask as a palettized PNG."""
    from PIL import Image
    img = Image.fromarray(mask, mode='L')
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    img.save(str(path))


def save_overlay(
    img_rgb: np.ndarray,
    mask: np.ndarray,
    path: Path,
    conf: np.ndarray | None = None,
    alpha: float = 0.50,
) -> None:
    """Save overlay with colour legend and statistics bar."""
    composite = overlay_with_legend(img_rgb, mask, alpha=alpha)
    composite = add_stat_bar(composite, mask, conf=conf)
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    bgr = cv2.cvtColor(composite, cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR)
    cv2.imwrite(str(path), bgr, [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 88])


def save_confidence_png(conf: np.ndarray, path: Path) -> None:
    """Save float32 confidence map as 8-bit PNG (0→0.0, 255→1.0)."""
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    cv2.imwrite(str(path), (conf * 255).clip(0, 255).astype(np.uint8))


def compute_class_fractions(mask: np.ndarray, n_classes: int) -> dict[str, float]:
    """Compute fraction of valid pixels belonging to each class."""
    valid = mask != IGNORE_INDEX
    total_valid = valid.sum()
    if total_valid == 0:
        return {CLASS_NAMES[i]: 0.0 for i in range(n_classes)}
    fractions = {}
    for i in range(n_classes):
        fractions[CLASS_NAMES[i]] = round(float((mask == i).sum() / total_valid), 4)
    return fractions


def find_unlabeled_images(unlabeled_dir: Path) -> list[Path]:
    exts = {'.jpg', '.jpeg', '.png', '.tif', '.tiff'}
    imgs = sorted([p for p in unlabeled_dir.rglob('*')
                   if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in exts])
    return imgs


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Main
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def label(
    config: dict,
    limit: int | None = None,
    no_crf: bool = False,
    shuffle: bool = False,
    confidence_override: float | None = None,
) -> None:
    device = torch.device('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu')
    print(f'Device: {device}')

    paths   = config['paths']
    cfg_pl  = config['pseudo_label']
    cfg_cls = config['classifier']

    out_dir = Path(paths['runs_dir']) / 'pseudo_labels'
    mask_dir      = out_dir / 'masks'
    overlay_dir   = out_dir / 'overlays'
    conf_dir      = out_dir / 'confidence'
    for d in [mask_dir, overlay_dir, conf_dir]:
        d.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Load classifier
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    ckpt_path = Path(paths['runs_dir']) / 'classifier' / 'best.pt'
    if not ckpt_path.exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError(
            f'Classifier checkpoint not found: {ckpt_path}\n'
            'Run src.classifier.train first.'
        )
    print(f'Loading classifier from {ckpt_path} ...')
    ckpt = torch.load(ckpt_path, map_location=device, weights_only=False)
    model = MultiscalePatchClassifier(
        num_classes=NUM_CLASSES,
        pretrained=False,
        img_size=cfg_cls['input_size'],
    ).to(device)
    model.load_state_dict(ckpt['model_state_dict'])
    model.eval()
    print(f'  Checkpoint val macro-F1: {ckpt.get("val_macro_f1", "N/A")}')

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Build sliding-window engine
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    engine = SlidingWindowInference(
        model=model,
        device=device,
        batch_size=config.get('pseudo_label_batch_size', 256),
        window_size=cfg_pl.get('window_size', 512),
        stride=cfg_pl.get('stride', 256),
        input_size=cfg_cls['input_size'],
        num_classes=NUM_CLASSES,
    )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Process images
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    unlabeled_dir = Path(paths['unlabeled_dir'])
    images = find_unlabeled_images(unlabeled_dir)
    if not images:
        raise FileNotFoundError(f'No images found in {unlabeled_dir}')
    if shuffle:
        import random
        random.Random(42).shuffle(images)
    if limit:
        images = images[:limit]

    print(f'\nFound {len(images)} unlabeled images. Processing...')
    conf_thresh = confidence_override if confidence_override is not None else cfg_pl['confidence_threshold']
    use_crf     = (not no_crf)
    print(f'Confidence threshold: {conf_thresh}')
    crf_iter    = cfg_pl['crf_iterations']

    manifest: list[dict] = []

    for img_path in tqdm(images, desc='Pseudo-labeling'):
        t0 = time.time()
        img_rgb = load_image(img_path)
        if img_rgb is None:
            print(f'  ⚠ Could not read {img_path.name} — skipping')
            continue

        # Two-pass sliding window
        prob_map = engine.run(img_rgb)           # (H, W, 7)

        # CRF + overrides + confidence threshold
        mask, conf = postprocess_probmap(
            img_rgb, prob_map, NUM_CLASSES,
            confidence_threshold=conf_thresh,
            use_crf=use_crf,
            crf_iter=crf_iter,
        )

        # Save outputs
        stem = img_path.stem
        save_mask_png(mask, mask_dir / f'{stem}.png')
        save_overlay(img_rgb, mask, overlay_dir / f'{stem}.jpg', conf=conf)
        save_confidence_png(conf, conf_dir / f'{stem}.png')

        # Record stats
        h, w      = mask.shape
        n_pixels  = h * w
        n_ignore  = int((mask == IGNORE_INDEX).sum())
        ignore_frac = round(n_ignore / n_pixels, 4)
        class_fracs = compute_class_fractions(mask, NUM_CLASSES)
        elapsed = round(time.time() - t0, 1)

        record = {
            'image':          img_path.name,
            'mask':           f'masks/{stem}.png',
            'overlay':        f'overlays/{stem}.jpg',
            'confidence':     f'confidence/{stem}.png',
            'height':         h,
            'width':          w,
            'ignore_fraction': ignore_frac,
            'class_fractions': class_fracs,
            'elapsed_s':      elapsed,
        }
        manifest.append(record)

        # Show all non-trivial classes
        cls_summary = ' '.join(
            f'{k[:4]}={v:.0%}'
            for k, v in class_fracs.items()
            if v >= 0.01 and k != 'background'
        )
        tqdm.write(f'  {img_path.name}: ignore={ignore_frac:.0%}  {cls_summary}  time={elapsed}s')

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Write manifest
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    manifest_path = out_dir / 'manifest.yaml'
    with open(manifest_path, 'w') as f:
        yaml.safe_dump({'pseudo_labels': manifest}, f,
                       default_flow_style=False, sort_keys=False)

    print(f'\nDone. Outputs: {out_dir}')
    print(f'Manifest:       {manifest_path}')
    print(f'Total images:   {len(manifest)}')

    # Summary statistics
    if manifest:
        avg_ignore   = sum(r['ignore_fraction'] for r in manifest) / len(manifest)
        avg_drought  = sum(r['class_fractions'].get('drought', 0) for r in manifest) / len(manifest)
        print(f'Avg ignore fraction:  {avg_ignore:.1%}')
        print(f'Avg drought fraction: {avg_drought:.1%}')


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Entry point
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument('--config', type=Path, default=Path('configs/config.yaml'))
    ap.add_argument('--limit', type=int, default=None,
                    help='Only process first N images (for testing)')
    ap.add_argument('--no-crf', action='store_true',
                    help='Skip CRF refinement (faster, lower quality)')
    ap.add_argument('--shuffle', action='store_true',
                    help='Pick random images instead of alphabetical first N')
    ap.add_argument('--confidence', type=float, default=None,
                    help='Override confidence threshold (default 0.55 from config)')
    ap.add_argument('--batch-size', type=int, default=256,
                    help='Tiles per GPU batch (default 256)')
    args = ap.parse_args()

    with open(args.config) as f:
        config = yaml.safe_load(f)

    config['pseudo_label_batch_size'] = args.batch_size

    label(
        config,
        limit=args.limit,
        no_crf=args.no_crf,
        shuffle=args.shuffle,
        confidence_override=args.confidence,
    )


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/pseudo_label/postprocess.py
================================================================================

"""
Post-processing for pseudo-label probability maps.

Two stages:
    1. Dense CRF refinement — uses RGB pairwise terms to snap class boundaries
       to actual color/texture edges in the image. Corrects soft blob-like
       boundaries produced by the sliding window.

    2. Structural-cue overrides — rule-based corrections for specific known
       error modes that the classifier consistently makes. Applied ONLY to
       pseudo-labels (not at orthomosaic inference time); they clean noisy
       labels before segmentation training.

Override rules (from the plan):
    A. Pixel labeled cotton_ball BUT local_std < threshold AND V > 0.9
       → likely bright soil or glare, not a boll → set to ignore
    B. Pixel labeled drought BUT FFT row score high AND surrounded by crop
       → likely inter-row soil in a healthy field → relabel soil
    C. Pixel labeled crop BUT ExG < -0.05 AND GLI < 0 everywhere in patch
       → likely shadow on soil misclassified as crop → set to ignore
    D. Pixel in glare_mask (very bright, desaturated) → set to ignore
       (any class — glare gives unreliable signal)
    E. Pixel labeled drought BUT ExG > 0.15
       → clearly green, not drought → relabel crop or green_weed
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import numpy as np
import pydensecrf.densecrf as dcrf

from src.preprocessing.indices import exg, gli
from src.preprocessing.masks import glare_mask
from src.preprocessing.tensor_builder import (
    _local_std,
    _fft_row_periodicity_blocked,
)
from src.utils.constants import CLASS_TO_IDX, IGNORE_INDEX


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Dense CRF
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def apply_crf(
    img_rgb: np.ndarray,
    prob_map: np.ndarray,
    n_classes: int,
    crf_iter: int = 5,
    gaussian_sxy: float = 3.0,
    gaussian_compat: float = 3.0,
    bilateral_sxy: float = 80.0,
    bilateral_srgb: float = 13.0,
    bilateral_compat: float = 10.0,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Refine a probability map with Dense CRF.

    Args:
        img_rgb:   (H, W, 3) uint8
        prob_map:  (H, W, n_classes) float32
        n_classes: number of classes

    Returns:
        (H, W) int32 refined class map
    """
    h, w = img_rgb.shape[:2]
    # CRF expects (n_classes, H*W) C-contiguous float32
    prob_flat = prob_map.reshape(-1, n_classes).T.astype(np.float32)
    prob_flat = np.clip(prob_flat, 1e-8, 1.0)
    unary = np.ascontiguousarray(-np.log(prob_flat))

    d = dcrf.DenseCRF2D(w, h, n_classes)
    d.setUnaryEnergy(unary)
    d.addPairwiseGaussian(sxy=gaussian_sxy, compat=gaussian_compat)
    d.addPairwiseBilateral(
        sxy=bilateral_sxy,
        srgb=bilateral_srgb,
        rgbim=np.ascontiguousarray(img_rgb),
        compat=bilateral_compat,
    )
    Q = np.array(d.inference(crf_iter)).reshape(n_classes, h, w)
    return Q.argmax(axis=0).astype(np.int32)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Structural-cue overrides
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def apply_structural_overrides(
    class_map: np.ndarray,
    prob_map: np.ndarray,
    img_rgb: np.ndarray,
    confidence_map: np.ndarray,
    local_std_thresh: float = 15.0,
    fft_row_thresh: float = 0.3,
    exg_drought_thresh: float = 0.15,
    exg_crop_thresh: float = -0.05,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Apply rule-based corrections to the class map.
    Sets unreliable pixels to IGNORE_INDEX.

    Args:
        class_map:        (H, W) int32 argmax predictions
        prob_map:         (H, W, n_classes) float32
        img_rgb:          (H, W, 3) uint8
        confidence_map:   (H, W) float32 — max probability per pixel
        *_thresh:         tuning thresholds

    Returns:
        (H, W) int32 with some pixels set to IGNORE_INDEX (255)
    """
    import cv2
    result = class_map.copy()
    h, w   = result.shape

    rgb01  = img_rgb.astype(np.float32) / 255.0
    gray   = cv2.cvtColor(img_rgb, cv2.COLOR_RGB2GRAY)
    hsv    = cv2.cvtColor(img_rgb, cv2.COLOR_RGB2HSV).astype(np.float32)
    v_norm = hsv[:, :, 2] / 255.0

    exg_map  = exg(rgb01)   # (H, W)
    gli_map  = gli(rgb01)   # (H, W)
    lstd_map = _local_std(gray, ksize=5)
    # local_std is normalized to [-1,1] in tensor_builder; recover absolute value
    lstd_abs = (lstd_map * 0.5 + 0.5) * 40.0   # approx inversion of normalization

    idx = CLASS_TO_IDX

    # ---- Rule A: cotton_ball in flat-brightness region → ignore ----
    cotton_mask = result == idx.get('cotton_ball', -1)
    flat_bright = (lstd_abs < local_std_thresh) & (v_norm > 0.90)
    result[cotton_mask & flat_bright] = IGNORE_INDEX

    # ---- Rule B: drought surrounded by crop with row structure → soil ----
    drought_mask = result == idx.get('drought', -1)
    if drought_mask.any():
        fft_map = _fft_row_periodicity_blocked(gray, block=256)
        # fft_map is in [-1,1]; convert back: row_score = (fft+1)/2 * 0.2
        row_score = (fft_map + 1.0) / 2.0 * 0.2
        high_row  = row_score > fft_row_thresh

        # Crop neighbourhood: dilate crop mask
        crop_mask = result == idx.get('crop', -1)
        kernel    = np.ones((15, 15), np.uint8)
        import cv2 as _cv2
        crop_dilated = _cv2.dilate(crop_mask.astype(np.uint8), kernel) > 0

        relabel_drought = drought_mask & high_row & crop_dilated
        soil_idx = idx.get('soil', 1)
        result[relabel_drought] = soil_idx

    # ---- Rule C: crop in region with no greenness → ignore ----
    crop_mask = result == idx.get('crop', -1)
    not_green = (exg_map < exg_crop_thresh) & (gli_map < 0.0)
    result[crop_mask & not_green] = IGNORE_INDEX

    # ---- Rule D: glare pixels → ignore (regardless of class) ----
    glare = glare_mask(img_rgb, v_thresh=0.95, s_thresh=0.10)
    result[glare] = IGNORE_INDEX

    # ---- Rule E: drought pixel is clearly green → relabel to crop ----
    drought_mask = result == idx.get('drought', -1)  # refreshed after rule B
    clearly_green = exg_map > exg_drought_thresh
    crop_idx = idx.get('crop', 2)
    result[drought_mask & clearly_green] = crop_idx

    return result


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Combined post-processing
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def postprocess_probmap(
    img_rgb: np.ndarray,
    prob_map: np.ndarray,
    n_classes: int,
    confidence_threshold: float = 0.55,
    use_crf: bool = True,
    crf_iter: int = 5,
    max_crf_pixels: int = 1024 * 1024,
) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
    """
    Full post-processing pipeline for one image's probability map.

    Args:
        max_crf_pixels: If the image has more pixels than this, CRF is
            automatically skipped (Dense CRF is O(N log N) and becomes
            impractically slow on large orthomosaic tiles). Default 1M px
            (~1024×1024). For full UAV images (8064×4536 = 36M px) set
            use_crf=False or process tiles individually.

    Returns:
        class_map:        (H, W) uint8 — predicted class per pixel,
                          IGNORE_INDEX (255) for low-confidence / overridden pixels
        confidence_map:   (H, W) float32 — max probability per pixel
    """
    confidence_map = prob_map.max(axis=2)   # (H, W)
    h, w = img_rgb.shape[:2]

    crf_active = use_crf and (h * w <= max_crf_pixels)
    if use_crf and not crf_active:
        import warnings
        warnings.warn(
            f'CRF skipped: image {h}×{w} ({h*w/1e6:.1f}M px) exceeds '
            f'max_crf_pixels={max_crf_pixels/1e6:.1f}M. '
            'Use --no-crf flag or reduce max_crf_pixels to suppress this warning.',
            stacklevel=2,
        )

    if crf_active:
        class_map = apply_crf(img_rgb, prob_map, n_classes,
                              crf_iter=crf_iter).astype(np.int32)
    else:
        class_map = prob_map.argmax(axis=2).astype(np.int32)

    # Low-confidence pixels → ignore
    class_map[confidence_map < confidence_threshold] = IGNORE_INDEX

    # Structural overrides
    class_map = apply_structural_overrides(
        class_map, prob_map, img_rgb, confidence_map
    )

    # Final clamp: any remaining out-of-range class → ignore
    valid = (class_map >= 0) & (class_map < n_classes)
    class_map[~valid & (class_map != IGNORE_INDEX)] = IGNORE_INDEX

    return class_map.astype(np.uint8), confidence_map




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/pseudo_label/sliding_window.py
================================================================================

"""
SlidingWindowInference — GPU-vectorised, full-image pseudo-labeling.

Design philosophy
-----------------
The original two-pass (256×256 stride 64 + 128×128 stride 32) approach
produced 43,000 Python-loop iterations per image.  Even on an H200 the
per-iteration overhead (numpy slice, cv2.resize, torch.from_numpy) dominated
at ~7 ms/tile → 300 s/image → months for 700 images.

This rewrite uses a single pass at 512×512 stride 256, which gives:
    - 480 tiles per 8064×4536 image  (90× fewer)
    - 2 GPU forward-pass batches at batch=256  (nearly zero GPU wait)
    - ~3–5 s/image on H200  (~60× faster)
    - BETTER pseudo-label quality: 512×512 crops contain 4× more spatial
      context than 256×256, which directly helps the drought↔soil and
      crop↔green_weed confusion pairs that limited the patch classifier.

Vectorisation strategy
----------------------
1. Build the 9-channel tensor ONCE per image on GPU (or CPU if needed).
2. Use torch.nn.Unfold to extract all tile crops simultaneously — one GPU op
   instead of 480 Python iterations.
3. Resize all tiles in a single F.interpolate call.
4. Forward the entire set in batches of 256.
5. Fold probability maps back with Hann-window weighting using torch.nn.Fold.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import math

import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
import torch.nn.functional as F

from src.preprocessing.tensor_builder import build_9channel


def _hann_window_2d(size: int) -> torch.Tensor:
    """
    2D Hann window as a float32 torch tensor (size, size).
    Clipped to minimum 0.01 so image-edge pixels always get nonzero weight.
    """
    h1d = torch.hann_window(size, periodic=False)
    h1d = torch.clamp(h1d, min=0.1)          # 1D min = 0.1
    h2d = torch.outer(h1d, h1d)               # 2D min = 0.01 at corners
    return h2d


class SlidingWindowInference:
    """
    Args:
        model:        MultiscalePatchClassifier (already on device).
        device:       torch.device.
        batch_size:   Tiles per GPU forward pass. 256 saturates H200.
        window_size:  Tile size in pixels (default 512).
        stride:       Stride between tiles (default 256 = 50% overlap).
        input_size:   Model's expected input size (tiles resized to this).
        num_classes:  Number of output classes.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        model: torch.nn.Module,
        device: torch.device,
        batch_size: int = 256,
        window_size: int = 512,
        stride: int = 256,
        input_size: int = 224,
        num_classes: int = 7,
        # Legacy params accepted but ignored (for backwards compat with config)
        window_sizes: list | None = None,
        strides: list | None = None,
        pass_weights: list | None = None,
        precompute_tensor: bool = True,
    ) -> None:
        self.model       = model
        self.device      = device
        self.batch_size  = batch_size
        self.window_size = window_size
        self.stride      = stride
        self.input_size  = input_size
        self.num_classes = num_classes
        model.eval()

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public API
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    @torch.no_grad()
    def run(self, img_rgb: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
        """
        Full inference on one large image.

        Args:
            img_rgb: (H, W, 3) uint8 RGB.

        Returns:
            (H, W, num_classes) float32 probability map.
        """
        H, W   = img_rgb.shape[:2]
        win    = self.window_size
        stride = self.stride
        C      = self.num_classes

        # ---- Step 1: build 9-channel tensor on GPU ----
        t9_cpu = build_9channel(img_rgb)            # (9, H, W) float32
        t9     = torch.from_numpy(t9_cpu).to(self.device)  # (9, H, W)

        # ---- Step 2: pad image so tiles cover edges cleanly ----
        # Pad right and bottom so (H_pad, W_pad) are exact multiples of stride
        pad_h = (math.ceil((H - win) / stride) * stride + win) - H
        pad_w = (math.ceil((W - win) / stride) * stride + win) - W
        # reflect-pad preserves local texture better than zero-pad at edges
        t9_pad = F.pad(t9.unsqueeze(0),
                       (0, pad_w, 0, pad_h), mode='reflect').squeeze(0)
        H_pad, W_pad = H + pad_h, W + pad_w

        # ---- Step 3: extract all tiles via unfold ----
        # unfold: (9, H_pad, W_pad) -> (9, n_h, n_w, win, win)
        n_h = (H_pad - win) // stride + 1
        n_w = (W_pad - win) // stride + 1

        # Use unfold along H then W
        tiles = t9_pad.unfold(1, win, stride).unfold(2, win, stride)
        # tiles: (9, n_h, n_w, win, win)
        tiles = tiles.permute(1, 2, 0, 3, 4).contiguous()
        # tiles: (n_h, n_w, 9, win, win)
        n_tiles = n_h * n_w
        tiles = tiles.view(n_tiles, 9, win, win)
        # tiles: (N, 9, win, win)

        # ---- Step 4: resize all tiles to model input size ----
        if win != self.input_size:
            tiles = F.interpolate(
                tiles, size=(self.input_size, self.input_size),
                mode='bilinear', align_corners=False
            )
        # tiles: (N, 9, input_size, input_size)

        # ---- Step 5: forward in batches ----
        orig_sizes = torch.full((self.batch_size,), win,
                                dtype=torch.long, device=self.device)
        all_probs  = []

        for start in range(0, n_tiles, self.batch_size):
            end   = min(start + self.batch_size, n_tiles)
            batch = tiles[start:end]                   # (B, 9, S, S)
            sz    = orig_sizes[:end - start]
            logits = self.model(batch, sz)             # (B, C)
            probs  = F.softmax(logits, dim=-1)         # (B, C)
            all_probs.append(probs.cpu())

        all_probs = torch.cat(all_probs, dim=0)        # (N, C)

        # ---- Step 6: fold probabilities back with Hann weighting ----
        hann = _hann_window_2d(win)                    # (win, win)
        # Expand Hann to (N, C, win, win) by outer product with class probs
        # Efficient: weight_map (H_pad, W_pad) and prob_map (C, H_pad, W_pad)
        # accumulated separately, then divide.

        prob_map    = torch.zeros(C, H_pad, W_pad, dtype=torch.float32)
        weight_map  = torch.zeros(1, H_pad, W_pad, dtype=torch.float32)
        hann_flat   = hann.view(1, win * win)          # (1, win²)

        tile_idx = 0
        for ih in range(n_h):
            for iw in range(n_w):
                y = ih * stride
                x = iw * stride
                p = all_probs[tile_idx]               # (C,)
                # Contribution: outer product hann * prob → (C, win, win)
                contrib = (p.unsqueeze(-1).unsqueeze(-1) *
                           hann.unsqueeze(0))          # (C, win, win)
                prob_map[:, y:y+win, x:x+win]   += contrib
                weight_map[0, y:y+win, x:x+win] += hann
                tile_idx += 1

        # Normalise by Hann weight
        weight_map = weight_map.clamp(min=1e-8)
        prob_map   = prob_map / weight_map             # (C, H_pad, W_pad)

        # Normalise along class axis (guards against floating-point drift)
        class_sum = prob_map.sum(dim=0, keepdim=True).clamp(min=1e-8)
        prob_map  = prob_map / class_sum               # (C, H_pad, W_pad)

        # ---- Step 7: crop back to original size ----
        prob_map = prob_map[:, :H, :W]                 # (C, H, W)

        # Return (H, W, C) float32 numpy
        return prob_map.permute(1, 2, 0).numpy()       # (H, W, C)




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/pseudo_label/viz.py
================================================================================

"""
Legend and overlay rendering utilities shared across pseudo-labeling outputs.

Provides:
    draw_legend_bar()     — returns a (H, W_legend, 3) legend strip that can be
                            concatenated onto the right side of any image.
    overlay_with_legend() — composite: image + colour overlay + legend strip.
    apply_palette()       — convert a class-index mask to an RGB colour mask.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import cv2
import numpy as np

from src.utils.constants import CLASS_NAMES, IGNORE_INDEX, NUM_CLASSES, PALETTE


# Minimum fraction of valid pixels for a class to appear in the legend
_LEGEND_MIN_FRAC: float = 0.001


def apply_palette(mask: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Convert a uint8 class-index mask to an RGB colour mask.

    Args:
        mask: (H, W) uint8 — values 0..NUM_CLASSES-1 or IGNORE_INDEX (255)

    Returns:
        (H, W, 3) uint8 RGB — ignore pixels are rendered as mid-grey (128,128,128)
    """
    rgb = np.zeros((*mask.shape, 3), dtype=np.uint8)
    for cls_idx, color in enumerate(PALETTE):
        rgb[mask == cls_idx] = color
    rgb[mask == IGNORE_INDEX] = (90, 90, 90)   # dark grey = "uncertain / ignored"
    return rgb


def draw_legend_bar(
    present_classes: list[int],
    bar_width: int = 180,
    row_height: int = 28,
    font_scale: float = 0.52,
    font_thickness: int = 1,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Build a vertical legend strip showing each present class as a colour swatch + label.

    Args:
        present_classes: list of class indices to show.
        bar_width:        pixel width of the legend strip.
        row_height:       pixel height per class row.

    Returns:
        (H_legend, bar_width, 3) uint8 RGB legend image.
    """
    # Always include ignore entry at the bottom
    rows = present_classes + [-1]
    h    = row_height * len(rows) + row_height   # top padding

    canvas = np.full((h, bar_width, 3), 240, dtype=np.uint8)   # light grey bg

    # Title
    cv2.putText(canvas, 'Classes', (8, row_height - 6),
                cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, font_scale + 0.05,
                (30, 30, 30), font_thickness + 1, cv2.LINE_AA)

    swatch_w = 26
    swatch_h = row_height - 8
    x_swatch = 8
    x_text   = swatch_w + 14
    margin   = 4

    for row_idx, cls_idx in enumerate(rows):
        y_top = row_height + row_idx * row_height

        if cls_idx == -1:
            # Ignore / uncertain entry
            color  = (90, 90, 90)
            label  = 'uncertain'
        else:
            color = tuple(int(v) for v in PALETTE[cls_idx])
            label = CLASS_NAMES[cls_idx]

        # Colour swatch (filled rectangle with thin black border)
        cv2.rectangle(canvas,
                      (x_swatch, y_top + margin),
                      (x_swatch + swatch_w, y_top + margin + swatch_h),
                      color, thickness=-1)
        cv2.rectangle(canvas,
                      (x_swatch, y_top + margin),
                      (x_swatch + swatch_w, y_top + margin + swatch_h),
                      (50, 50, 50), thickness=1)

        # Class label text
        cv2.putText(canvas, label,
                    (x_text, y_top + margin + swatch_h - 4),
                    cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, font_scale,
                    (30, 30, 30), font_thickness, cv2.LINE_AA)

    return canvas


def overlay_with_legend(
    img_rgb: np.ndarray,
    mask: np.ndarray,
    alpha: float = 0.50,
    min_frac: float = _LEGEND_MIN_FRAC,
    max_display_size: int = 2048,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Produce a single image: RGB overlay + legend strip on the right.

    Args:
        img_rgb:           (H, W, 3) uint8 original image.
        mask:              (H, W) uint8 class-index mask (IGNORE_INDEX = uncertain).
        alpha:             Opacity of the class colour overlay.
        min_frac:          Classes covering less than this fraction are omitted from legend.
        max_display_size:  Longest side is capped at this for readable output.

    Returns:
        (H_out, W_out + legend_width, 3) uint8 composite.
    """
    H, W = img_rgb.shape[:2]

    # Determine present classes for the legend
    valid      = mask != IGNORE_INDEX
    n_valid    = max(valid.sum(), 1)
    present    = [
        cls_idx for cls_idx in range(NUM_CLASSES)
        if (mask == cls_idx).sum() / n_valid >= min_frac
    ]

    # Colour overlay
    color_mask = apply_palette(mask)
    overlay    = img_rgb.copy()
    overlay[valid] = (
        alpha * color_mask[valid].astype(np.float32) +
        (1.0 - alpha) * img_rgb[valid].astype(np.float32)
    ).astype(np.uint8)
    # Ignored pixels: tint grey slightly so the user knows they exist
    ignore     = mask == IGNORE_INDEX
    overlay[ignore] = (
        0.25 * np.array([90,90,90], dtype=np.float32) +
        0.75 * img_rgb[ignore].astype(np.float32)
    ).astype(np.uint8)

    # Legend bar — height must match the overlay
    legend  = draw_legend_bar(present, bar_width=180, row_height=max(24, H // 25))
    leg_h   = legend.shape[0]
    if leg_h < H:
        pad     = np.full((H - leg_h, legend.shape[1], 3), 240, dtype=np.uint8)
        legend  = np.vstack([legend, pad])
    else:
        legend  = legend[:H]

    composite = np.hstack([overlay, legend])

    # Resize if very large (for quick visual inspection)
    ch, cw = composite.shape[:2]
    if max(ch, cw) > max_display_size:
        scale = max_display_size / max(ch, cw)
        composite = cv2.resize(composite,
                               (int(cw * scale), int(ch * scale)),
                               interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)

    return composite


def add_stat_bar(
    composite: np.ndarray,
    mask: np.ndarray,
    conf: np.ndarray | None = None,
    bar_height: int = 36,
    font_scale: float = 0.48,
) -> np.ndarray:
    """
    Append a one-line statistics bar at the bottom of a composite image.
    Shows per-class percentages + ignore fraction + mean confidence.

    Args:
        composite: (H, W, 3) composite image (from overlay_with_legend).
        mask:      (H, W) original full-resolution mask (before any resize).
        conf:      (H, W) float32 confidence map, or None.
    """
    H, W = composite.shape[:2]
    bar  = np.full((bar_height, W, 3), 50, dtype=np.uint8)   # dark bg

    valid     = mask != IGNORE_INDEX
    n_pixels  = mask.size
    n_valid   = max(valid.sum(), 1)
    ignore_pc = (mask == IGNORE_INDEX).sum() / n_pixels * 100

    parts = [f'ignore={ignore_pc:.0f}%']
    for cls_idx in range(NUM_CLASSES):
        frac = (mask == cls_idx).sum() / n_valid
        if frac >= 0.01:
            name = CLASS_NAMES[cls_idx][:5]
            parts.append(f'{name}={frac:.0%}')

    if conf is not None:
        mean_conf = conf[valid].mean() if valid.any() else 0.0
        parts.append(f'mean_conf={mean_conf:.2f}')

    text = '  |  '.join(parts)
    cv2.putText(bar, text, (8, bar_height - 10),
                cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX, font_scale,
                (220, 220, 220), 1, cv2.LINE_AA)

    return np.vstack([composite, bar])

================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/segmentation/compositor.py
================================================================================

"""
Synthetic compositor for segmentation training.

Generates (image, mask) pairs by pasting labeled patches from data/patches/
onto random crops from data/unlabeled/.

Why this is critical
--------------------
The patch classifier hit a ceiling because patches lack spatial context.
The segmentation model needs to see classes ADJACENT to one another in
realistic configurations:
    - drought patch next to soil patch  → teaches the drought/soil boundary
    - drought patch next to dry_weed    → teaches drought/dry_weed
    - green_weed next to crop           → teaches weed/crop separation
    - small cotton_ball on soil         → teaches small-object detection

These adjacencies are RARE in raw pseudo-labels (the classifier already
struggles there) and HARD to find in the small clean set (only 25 tiles).
Synthetic composites provide unlimited training examples of exactly these
hard cases.

Design choices
--------------
- 60% of composites force one of the 5 confusion pairs side-by-side.
- 40% are random multi-class compositions.
- Feathered alpha edges (Gaussian sigma=5) prevent the model from learning
  to detect paste seams instead of real class boundaries.
- Patch sizes randomized within a realistic range (64-256 px on 512-px tile).
- Background tiles are randomly cropped from unlabeled/ to preserve
  domain-realistic illumination, soil colour, lens characteristics.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import random
from pathlib import Path

import cv2
import numpy as np

from src.utils.constants import (
    CLASS_NAMES,
    CLASS_TO_IDX,
    CONFUSION_PAIRS,
    IGNORE_INDEX,
    NUM_CLASSES,
    PATCH_FOLDER_TO_CLASS,
)

# Tile size composites are generated at
DEFAULT_TILE_SIZE = 512
# Patch sizes in pixels (will be picked at random)
PATCH_SIZE_RANGE = (64, 256)
# Special small-patch range for cotton_ball (model misses small ones)
COTTON_SMALL_RANGE = (16, 80)
# How many patches per composite
N_PATCHES_RANGE = (3, 7)
# Probability of forcing a confusion-pair composition
CONFUSION_PAIR_PROB = 0.60
# Probability of forcing a small-cotton composition
SMALL_COTTON_PROB = 0.20
# Feather sigma for paste edges
FEATHER_SIGMA = 5.0


class SyntheticCompositor:
    """
    Builds (image, mask) training pairs on the fly.

    Args:
        patches_dir:   data/patches root (folders per class).
        backgrounds_dir: data/unlabeled root for background tiles.
        tile_size:     output square size.
        seed:          optional fixed seed for reproducibility.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        patches_dir: str | Path,
        backgrounds_dir: str | Path,
        tile_size: int = DEFAULT_TILE_SIZE,
        seed: int | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self.patches_dir   = Path(patches_dir)
        self.backgrounds_dir = Path(backgrounds_dir)
        self.tile_size     = tile_size
        self.rng           = random.Random(seed)

        # Index all patches by class
        self._patches_by_class: dict[int, list[Path]] = {}
        for folder, cls_name in PATCH_FOLDER_TO_CLASS.items():
            cls_idx = CLASS_TO_IDX.get(cls_name)
            if cls_idx is None:
                continue
            folder_path = self.patches_dir / folder
            if not folder_path.is_dir():
                continue
            exts = {'.png', '.jpg', '.jpeg', '.tif', '.tiff'}
            files = [p for p in folder_path.rglob('*') if p.suffix.lower() in exts]
            self._patches_by_class[cls_idx] = files

        if not self._patches_by_class:
            raise FileNotFoundError(
                f"No patches found under {patches_dir}. Check folder names."
            )

        # Index all background images
        bg_exts = {'.jpg', '.jpeg', '.png', '.tif', '.tiff'}
        self._bg_files = [
            p for p in self.backgrounds_dir.rglob('*')
            if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in bg_exts
        ]
        if not self._bg_files:
            raise FileNotFoundError(f"No backgrounds found under {backgrounds_dir}")

        # Confusion pairs as class indices
        self._confusion_pairs_idx = [
            (CLASS_TO_IDX[a], CLASS_TO_IDX[b])
            for a, b in CONFUSION_PAIRS
            if a in CLASS_TO_IDX and b in CLASS_TO_IDX
        ]

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Public
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def sample(self) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
        """
        Generate one (image, mask) composite.

        Returns:
            image: (H, W, 3) uint8 RGB
            mask:  (H, W) uint8 — pixel values 0..6 (and 255 over un-pasted
                   background, treated as ignore so the model focuses on
                   the deliberate paste regions).
        """
        bg = self._load_background()
        # Mask starts as IGNORE everywhere — pasted patches will overwrite
        mask = np.full(bg.shape[:2], IGNORE_INDEX, dtype=np.uint8)

        # Decide composition type
        r = self.rng.random()
        if r < CONFUSION_PAIR_PROB:
            self._paste_confusion_pair(bg, mask)
        elif r < CONFUSION_PAIR_PROB + SMALL_COTTON_PROB:
            self._paste_small_cotton(bg, mask)
        else:
            self._paste_random_mix(bg, mask)

        return bg, mask

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Composition strategies
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _paste_confusion_pair(self, bg: np.ndarray, mask: np.ndarray) -> None:
        """Paste both classes of a confusion pair adjacent to each other."""
        cls_a, cls_b = self.rng.choice(self._confusion_pairs_idx)

        # Skip if either class has no patches
        if cls_a not in self._patches_by_class or cls_b not in self._patches_by_class:
            self._paste_random_mix(bg, mask)
            return

        # Pick a random center
        cx = self.rng.randint(self.tile_size // 4, 3 * self.tile_size // 4)
        cy = self.rng.randint(self.tile_size // 4, 3 * self.tile_size // 4)

        # Patch A on the left/top, Patch B on the right/bottom (random orientation)
        sz_a = self.rng.randint(*PATCH_SIZE_RANGE)
        sz_b = self.rng.randint(*PATCH_SIZE_RANGE)

        if self.rng.random() < 0.5:
            # Horizontal split
            x_a = max(0, cx - sz_a)
            y_a = max(0, cy - sz_a // 2)
            x_b = min(self.tile_size - sz_b, cx)
            y_b = max(0, cy - sz_b // 2)
        else:
            # Vertical split
            x_a = max(0, cx - sz_a // 2)
            y_a = max(0, cy - sz_a)
            x_b = max(0, cx - sz_b // 2)
            y_b = min(self.tile_size - sz_b, cy)

        self._paste_one(bg, mask, cls_a, sz_a, x_a, y_a)
        self._paste_one(bg, mask, cls_b, sz_b, x_b, y_b)

        # Add 1-3 more random patches to make it look like a real field
        for _ in range(self.rng.randint(1, 3)):
            cls = self.rng.choice(list(self._patches_by_class.keys()))
            sz  = self.rng.randint(*PATCH_SIZE_RANGE)
            x   = self.rng.randint(0, self.tile_size - sz)
            y   = self.rng.randint(0, self.tile_size - sz)
            self._paste_one(bg, mask, cls, sz, x, y)

    def _paste_small_cotton(self, bg: np.ndarray, mask: np.ndarray) -> None:
        """Generate composites with small cotton_ball patches on soil/crop bg."""
        cotton_idx = CLASS_TO_IDX.get('cotton_ball', 3)
        crop_idx   = CLASS_TO_IDX.get('crop', 2)
        soil_idx   = CLASS_TO_IDX.get('soil', 1)

        if cotton_idx not in self._patches_by_class:
            self._paste_random_mix(bg, mask)
            return

        # Paste a base crop or soil region
        for _ in range(self.rng.randint(1, 3)):
            cls = self.rng.choice([crop_idx, soil_idx])
            if cls not in self._patches_by_class:
                continue
            sz  = self.rng.randint(150, 300)
            x   = self.rng.randint(0, self.tile_size - sz)
            y   = self.rng.randint(0, self.tile_size - sz)
            self._paste_one(bg, mask, cls, sz, x, y)

        # Sprinkle small cotton balls
        n_cotton = self.rng.randint(3, 10)
        for _ in range(n_cotton):
            sz = self.rng.randint(*COTTON_SMALL_RANGE)
            x  = self.rng.randint(0, self.tile_size - sz)
            y  = self.rng.randint(0, self.tile_size - sz)
            self._paste_one(bg, mask, cotton_idx, sz, x, y)

    def _paste_random_mix(self, bg: np.ndarray, mask: np.ndarray) -> None:
        """Paste 3-7 random-class patches anywhere on the tile."""
        n = self.rng.randint(*N_PATCHES_RANGE)
        for _ in range(n):
            cls = self.rng.choice(list(self._patches_by_class.keys()))
            sz  = self.rng.randint(*PATCH_SIZE_RANGE)
            x   = self.rng.randint(0, self.tile_size - sz)
            y   = self.rng.randint(0, self.tile_size - sz)
            self._paste_one(bg, mask, cls, sz, x, y)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Low-level paste
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------

    def _paste_one(
        self,
        bg: np.ndarray,
        mask: np.ndarray,
        cls_idx: int,
        size: int,
        x: int,
        y: int,
    ) -> None:
        """Paste one patch into bg at (x, y) with feathered edges + update mask."""
        files = self._patches_by_class.get(cls_idx)
        if not files:
            return
        path = self.rng.choice(files)
        patch = cv2.imread(str(path), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
        if patch is None:
            return
        patch = cv2.cvtColor(patch, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
        # Resize to target size
        patch = cv2.resize(patch, (size, size), interpolation=cv2.INTER_LINEAR)

        # Build feathered alpha
        alpha = np.ones((size, size), dtype=np.float32)
        feather = max(3, int(size * 0.08))
        # Linear fade from border
        for i in range(feather):
            v = i / feather
            alpha[i, :]            = np.minimum(alpha[i, :], v)
            alpha[-1-i, :]         = np.minimum(alpha[-1-i, :], v)
            alpha[:, i]            = np.minimum(alpha[:, i], v)
            alpha[:, -1-i]         = np.minimum(alpha[:, -1-i], v)
        # Smooth with Gaussian
        alpha = cv2.GaussianBlur(alpha, (0, 0), sigmaX=FEATHER_SIGMA)
        alpha = alpha.clip(0, 1)

        # Bounds check
        h, w = bg.shape[:2]
        x2 = min(x + size, w)
        y2 = min(y + size, h)
        psz_x = x2 - x
        psz_y = y2 - y
        if psz_x <= 0 or psz_y <= 0:
            return
        patch = patch[:psz_y, :psz_x]
        alpha = alpha[:psz_y, :psz_x]

        # Composite: bg' = alpha * patch + (1-alpha) * bg
        a3 = alpha[..., None]
        bg[y:y2, x:x2] = (
            a3 * patch.astype(np.float32) +
            (1 - a3) * bg[y:y2, x:x2].astype(np.float32)
        ).astype(np.uint8)

        # Update mask wherever alpha is meaningfully nonzero (avoid edge blur in mask)
        # Use a stricter threshold so mask doesn't bleed into background
        update = alpha > 0.5
        mask[y:y2, x:x2][update] = cls_idx

    def _load_background(self) -> np.ndarray:
        """Load a random TILE_SIZE crop from a random unlabeled image."""
        for _ in range(10):                          # retry on read failures
            path = self.rng.choice(self._bg_files)
            img = cv2.imread(str(path), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
            if img is None:
                continue
            img = cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
            h, w = img.shape[:2]
            if h < self.tile_size or w < self.tile_size:
                # Image too small — resize up
                scale = max(self.tile_size / h, self.tile_size / w) * 1.05
                img   = cv2.resize(img, (int(w * scale), int(h * scale)),
                                   interpolation=cv2.INTER_LINEAR)
                h, w = img.shape[:2]
            y = self.rng.randint(0, h - self.tile_size)
            x = self.rng.randint(0, w - self.tile_size)
            return img[y:y+self.tile_size, x:x+self.tile_size].copy()
        # Fallback if we couldn't read any: synthetic noise
        return np.random.randint(60, 180, (self.tile_size, self.tile_size, 3),
                                 dtype=np.uint8)




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/segmentation/dataset.py
================================================================================

"""
Segmentation training dataset.

Combines three sources, each contributing per-batch in fixed proportions:

    CLEAN:      data/clean_masks/ (hand-corrected)
                + corresponding tiles from data/clean_images/
                Loss weight: 5.0 (high confidence — gold standard)

    PSEUDO:     runs/pseudo_labels/masks/ (noisy classifier predictions)
                + corresponding originals from data/unlabeled/
                Loss weight: 1.0 (large quantity, noisy)

    COMPOSITE:  generated on the fly by SyntheticCompositor
                Loss weight: 1.0 (unlimited, exact masks)

All sources return the same dict format:
    {
        'image':       (9, H, W) float32 tensor (RGB + 5 indices + V)
        'mask':        (H, W)    int64    class indices (IGNORE_INDEX=255 for ignored)
        'source':      str       'clean' | 'pseudo' | 'composite'
        'loss_weight': float     per-sample loss weight
        'path':        str       source path for debugging
    }
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import random
from pathlib import Path

import cv2
import numpy as np
import torch
from torch.utils.data import Dataset

from src.preprocessing.tensor_builder import build_9channel
from src.segmentation.compositor import SyntheticCompositor
from src.utils.constants import IGNORE_INDEX, NUM_CLASSES


# Per-source loss weights (clean is more trustworthy → higher weight)
# Higher clean weight reflects: pseudo-labels are heavily biased toward
# 'crop' (~84%) while clean masks accurately reflect the field composition
# (soil 64%, crop 5%, etc). Pseudo-labels get a low weight so they don't
# pull soil predictions toward 'crop'.
LOSS_WEIGHTS = {
    'clean':     8.0,
    'pseudo':    0.5,
    'composite': 1.0,
}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Augmentation
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _augment_image_and_mask(
    img: np.ndarray,
    mask: np.ndarray,
    crop_size: int,
    train: bool,
) -> tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
    """
    Geometric and color augmentation. For training, applies random crop
    + flips + 90° rotations + HSV jitter. For val, just centre-crops.
    """
    h, w = img.shape[:2]

    if train:
        # Random crop
        if h > crop_size:
            y = random.randint(0, h - crop_size)
        else:
            y = 0
        if w > crop_size:
            x = random.randint(0, w - crop_size)
        else:
            x = 0
    else:
        # Centre crop
        y = max(0, (h - crop_size) // 2)
        x = max(0, (w - crop_size) // 2)

    # Crop (and pad with reflection if smaller than crop_size)
    img  = img[y:y+crop_size, x:x+crop_size]
    mask = mask[y:y+crop_size, x:x+crop_size]
    if img.shape[0] < crop_size or img.shape[1] < crop_size:
        pad_y = crop_size - img.shape[0]
        pad_x = crop_size - img.shape[1]
        img  = cv2.copyMakeBorder(img,  0, pad_y, 0, pad_x,
                                  cv2.BORDER_REFLECT_101)
        mask = cv2.copyMakeBorder(mask, 0, pad_y, 0, pad_x,
                                  cv2.BORDER_CONSTANT,
                                  value=IGNORE_INDEX)

    if train:
        # Flips
        if random.random() < 0.5:
            img  = np.ascontiguousarray(img[:, ::-1])
            mask = np.ascontiguousarray(mask[:, ::-1])
        if random.random() < 0.5:
            img  = np.ascontiguousarray(img[::-1, :])
            mask = np.ascontiguousarray(mask[::-1, :])
        # 90° rotations
        k = random.randint(0, 3)
        if k:
            img  = np.ascontiguousarray(np.rot90(img,  k))
            mask = np.ascontiguousarray(np.rot90(mask, k))
        # Mild HSV jitter
        if random.random() < 0.5:
            hsv = cv2.cvtColor(img, cv2.COLOR_RGB2HSV).astype(np.int16)
            hsv[..., 0] = (hsv[..., 0] + random.randint(-8, 8)) % 180
            hsv[..., 1] = np.clip(hsv[..., 1] + random.randint(-25, 25), 0, 255)
            hsv[..., 2] = np.clip(hsv[..., 2] + random.randint(-25, 25), 0, 255)
            img = cv2.cvtColor(hsv.astype(np.uint8), cv2.COLOR_HSV2RGB)

    return img, mask


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Sub-datasets
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class CleanMaskDataset(Dataset):
    """Hand-corrected (image, mask) pairs."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        images_dir: str | Path,
        masks_dir: str | Path,
        crop_size: int = 512,
        train: bool = True,
    ) -> None:
        self.images_dir = Path(images_dir)
        self.masks_dir  = Path(masks_dir)
        self.crop_size  = crop_size
        self.train      = train

        # Pair files by stem
        mask_files = sorted(self.masks_dir.glob('*.png'))
        self.pairs: list[tuple[Path, Path]] = []
        for m in mask_files:
            # Image could be .jpg or .png
            for ext in ('.jpg', '.jpeg', '.png'):
                img = self.images_dir / (m.stem + ext)
                if img.exists():
                    self.pairs.append((img, m))
                    break

        if not self.pairs:
            raise FileNotFoundError(
                f'No (image, mask) pairs in {images_dir} ↔ {masks_dir}. '
                'Run scripts/prepare_clean_set.py first.'
            )

    def __len__(self) -> int:
        return len(self.pairs)

    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> dict:
        img_path, mask_path = self.pairs[idx]
        img_bgr = cv2.imread(str(img_path), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
        img     = cv2.cvtColor(img_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
        mask    = cv2.imread(str(mask_path), cv2.IMREAD_GRAYSCALE)

        img, mask = _augment_image_and_mask(img, mask, self.crop_size, self.train)
        return {
            'image':       torch.from_numpy(build_9channel(img)),
            'mask':        torch.from_numpy(mask.astype(np.int64)),
            'source':      'clean',
            'loss_weight': LOSS_WEIGHTS['clean'],
            'path':        str(img_path),
        }


class PseudoLabelDataset(Dataset):
    """Pseudo-labeled (image, mask) pairs from the full unlabeled run."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        images_dir: str | Path,
        masks_dir: str | Path,
        crop_size: int = 512,
        train: bool = True,
    ) -> None:
        self.images_dir = Path(images_dir)
        self.masks_dir  = Path(masks_dir)
        self.crop_size  = crop_size
        self.train      = train

        mask_files = sorted(self.masks_dir.glob('*.png'))
        self.pairs: list[tuple[Path, Path]] = []
        for m in mask_files:
            for ext in ('.jpg', '.jpeg', '.png'):
                img = self.images_dir / (m.stem + ext)
                if img.exists():
                    self.pairs.append((img, m))
                    break

        if not self.pairs:
            raise FileNotFoundError(
                f'No (image, mask) pairs in {images_dir} ↔ {masks_dir}. '
                'Run pseudo-labeling first.'
            )

    def __len__(self) -> int:
        return len(self.pairs)

    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> dict:
        img_path, mask_path = self.pairs[idx]
        img_bgr = cv2.imread(str(img_path), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
        img     = cv2.cvtColor(img_bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
        mask    = cv2.imread(str(mask_path), cv2.IMREAD_GRAYSCALE)

        # Random crop from this large image
        img, mask = _augment_image_and_mask(img, mask, self.crop_size, self.train)
        return {
            'image':       torch.from_numpy(build_9channel(img)),
            'mask':        torch.from_numpy(mask.astype(np.int64)),
            'source':      'pseudo',
            'loss_weight': LOSS_WEIGHTS['pseudo'],
            'path':        str(img_path),
        }


class CompositeDataset(Dataset):
    """On-the-fly synthetic composites. Returns a fixed virtual length per epoch."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        patches_dir: str | Path,
        backgrounds_dir: str | Path,
        crop_size: int = 512,
        virtual_length: int = 1000,
        train: bool = True,
        seed: int | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        self.compositor = SyntheticCompositor(
            patches_dir=patches_dir,
            backgrounds_dir=backgrounds_dir,
            tile_size=crop_size,
            seed=seed,
        )
        self.crop_size      = crop_size
        self.virtual_length = virtual_length
        self.train          = train

    def __len__(self) -> int:
        return self.virtual_length

    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> dict:
        img, mask = self.compositor.sample()
        # Composites already at crop_size; still apply train-time flips/HSV
        if self.train:
            img, mask = _augment_image_and_mask(img, mask, self.crop_size, train=True)
        return {
            'image':       torch.from_numpy(build_9channel(img)),
            'mask':        torch.from_numpy(mask.astype(np.int64)),
            'source':      'composite',
            'loss_weight': LOSS_WEIGHTS['composite'],
            'path':        'synthetic',
        }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Mixed dataset / sampler
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class MixedSegmentationDataset(Dataset):
    """
    Mixes the three sources at fixed ratios per "virtual" sample index.

    With ratios (clean=4, pseudo=4, composite=8) in a batch of 16, every
    full pass through this dataset draws each source proportionally.
    The composite source effectively oversamples the clean+pseudo sources
    by repeating their indices.
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        clean: CleanMaskDataset | None,
        pseudo: PseudoLabelDataset | None,
        composite: CompositeDataset | None,
        ratio_clean: int = 4,
        ratio_pseudo: int = 4,
        ratio_composite: int = 8,
    ) -> None:
        self.sources    : list[Dataset]       = []
        self.weights    : list[int]           = []
        if clean is not None and len(clean) > 0:
            self.sources.append(clean); self.weights.append(ratio_clean)
        if pseudo is not None and len(pseudo) > 0:
            self.sources.append(pseudo); self.weights.append(ratio_pseudo)
        if composite is not None and len(composite) > 0:
            self.sources.append(composite); self.weights.append(ratio_composite)
        assert self.sources, "MixedSegmentationDataset has no sources"

        # Compute virtual length: large enough that one epoch passes
        # through composite ~once and respects the ratios
        composite_len = (len(composite) if composite is not None else
                         max(len(s) for s in self.sources))
        total_ratio = sum(self.weights)
        self.virtual_length = (
            composite_len * total_ratio // max(ratio_composite, 1)
        )

        # Build sampling indices, refreshed at each training epoch.
        self.set_epoch(0)

    def set_epoch(self, epoch: int) -> None:
        total_ratio = sum(self.weights)
        self._table: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
        for src_idx, (src, w) in enumerate(zip(self.sources, self.weights)):
            n_appear = (self.virtual_length * w) // total_ratio
            offset = (epoch * n_appear) % len(src)
            for i in range(n_appear):
                self._table.append((src_idx, (offset + i) % len(src)))
        random.Random(epoch).shuffle(self._table)
        self.virtual_length = len(self._table)

    def __len__(self) -> int:
        return self.virtual_length

    def __getitem__(self, idx: int) -> dict:
        src_idx, sub_idx = self._table[idx]
        return self.sources[src_idx][sub_idx]




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/segmentation/eval_calibration_v11.py

"""
SegFormer-B2 training loop.

Usage:
    python -m src.segmentation.train --config configs/config.yaml

What it does:
    1. Builds mixed dataset (clean + pseudo + synthetic).
    2. Loads SegFormer-B2 with 9-channel input adapter.
    3. Computes per-class loss weights from training pixel counts.
    4. Trains with CE + Dice + Lovász, with per-sample loss weights.
    5. Validates each epoch on held-out clean tiles.
    6. Saves best mIoU checkpoint, writes training_report.json.
    7. Dumps val_predictions/ with PNG overlays of best-epoch predictions.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import time
from pathlib import Path

import cv2
import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter
from tqdm import tqdm

from src.preprocessing.tensor_builder import build_9channel
from src.segmentation.dataset import (
    CleanMaskDataset,
    CompositeDataset,
    MixedSegmentationDataset,
    PseudoLabelDataset,
    _augment_image_and_mask,
)
from src.segmentation.losses import SegmentationLoss, make_class_weights
from src.segmentation.model import SegFormerB2
from src.utils.constants import (
    CLASS_NAMES,
    CLASS_TO_IDX,
    IGNORE_INDEX,
    NUM_CLASSES,
    PALETTE,
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def collate_fn(batch: list[dict]) -> dict:
    """Stack the dicts from PatchDataset → batched dict."""
    images       = torch.stack([b['image'] for b in batch])
    masks        = torch.stack([b['mask']  for b in batch])
    weights      = torch.tensor([b['loss_weight'] for b in batch],
                                dtype=torch.float32)
    sources      = [b['source'] for b in batch]
    return {
        'image':         images,
        'mask':          masks,
        'sample_weight': weights,
        'source':        sources,
    }


def compute_iou_per_class(
    confusion: np.ndarray,
) -> tuple[np.ndarray, float]:
    """
    Per-class IoU from a (num_classes, num_classes) confusion matrix
    where confusion[true, pred] = pixel count.
    """
    iou_per = np.zeros(NUM_CLASSES, dtype=np.float64)
    for c in range(NUM_CLASSES):
        tp = confusion[c, c]
        fp = confusion[:, c].sum() - tp
        fn = confusion[c, :].sum() - tp
        denom = tp + fp + fn
        iou_per[c] = tp / denom if denom > 0 else np.nan
    miou = np.nanmean(iou_per)
    return iou_per, miou


def update_confusion(
    confusion: np.ndarray,
    preds: torch.Tensor,
    targets: torch.Tensor,
    num_classes: int,
) -> None:
    """Add to confusion matrix in-place. preds/targets are torch (B, H, W)."""
    valid = (targets != IGNORE_INDEX)
    p = preds[valid].cpu().numpy().astype(np.int32)
    t = targets[valid].cpu().numpy().astype(np.int32)
    # bincount approach is fast
    idx = t * num_classes + p
    counts = np.bincount(idx, minlength=num_classes * num_classes)
    confusion += counts.reshape(num_classes, num_classes)


def save_prediction_overlay(
    img_rgb: np.ndarray,
    true_mask: np.ndarray,
    pred_mask: np.ndarray,
    save_path: Path,
) -> None:
    """Save a 3-panel comparison: original | true mask | predicted mask."""
    H, W = img_rgb.shape[:2]

    def colorize(m):
        out = np.zeros((*m.shape, 3), dtype=np.uint8)
        for i, c in enumerate(PALETTE):
            out[m == i] = c
        out[m == IGNORE_INDEX] = (90, 90, 90)
        return out

    true_color = colorize(true_mask)
    pred_color = colorize(pred_mask)

    # Side-by-side
    composite = np.hstack([img_rgb, true_color, pred_color])

    # Label bar at top
    bar = np.full((30, composite.shape[1], 3), 230, dtype=np.uint8)
    font = cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX
    cv2.putText(bar, 'Original', (10, 22), font, 0.55, (20,20,20), 1, cv2.LINE_AA)
    cv2.putText(bar, 'Ground truth', (W + 10, 22), font, 0.55, (20,20,20), 1, cv2.LINE_AA)
    cv2.putText(bar, 'Prediction', (2*W + 10, 22), font, 0.55, (20,20,20), 1, cv2.LINE_AA)
    composite = np.vstack([bar, composite])

    save_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    cv2.imwrite(str(save_path), cv2.cvtColor(composite, cv2.COLOR_RGB2BGR),
                [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 88])


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Train
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def train(config: dict, resume_path=None) -> None:
    import os
    device = torch.device('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu')
    print(f'Device: {device}')
    if torch.cuda.is_available():
        print(f'GPU:    {torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)}')
        print(f'VRAM:   {torch.cuda.get_device_properties(0).total_memory/1e9:.1f} GB')
    slurm_keys = ['SLURM_JOB_ID','SLURM_JOB_NAME','SLURM_NODELIST','SLURM_CPUS_PER_TASK']
    slurm_info = {k: os.environ[k] for k in slurm_keys if k in os.environ}
    if slurm_info:
        print('SLURM:', slurm_info)

    paths   = config['paths']
    cfg_seg = config['segmentation']

    out_dir = Path(paths['runs_dir']) / 'segmentation'
    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    writer = SummaryWriter(log_dir=out_dir / cfg_seg.get('run_name', 'default') / 'tb_logs')

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Datasets
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    crop_size = cfg_seg['input_size']

    clean_images_dir = Path(paths.get('clean_images_dir',
                                      'data/clean_images'))
    clean_masks_dir  = Path(paths.get('clean_masks_dir',
                                      'data/clean_masks'))

    # Stratified train/val split: ensure drought-rich tiles go to val
    all_clean_masks = sorted(
        m for m in clean_masks_dir.glob('*.png')
        if any(
            (clean_images_dir / (m.stem + ext)).is_file()
            for ext in ('.png', '.jpg', '.jpeg', '.tif', '.tiff',
                        '.PNG', '.JPG', '.JPEG', '.TIF', '.TIFF')
        )
    )
    if len(all_clean_masks) < 10:
        print(f'[WARNING] Only {len(all_clean_masks)} clean masks found. '
              'Validation will be unreliable.')
    val_n = max(3, int(len(all_clean_masks) * 0.2))

    # Compute drought fraction per tile for stratification
    drought_fracs = {}
    for m in all_clean_masks:
        mask_arr = cv2.imread(str(m), cv2.IMREAD_GRAYSCALE)
        valid    = mask_arr != 255
        n_valid  = max(valid.sum(), 1)
        drought_fracs[m.stem] = float((mask_arr == 6).sum()) / n_valid

    # Sort by drought fraction descending, interleave into val/train
    sorted_stems = sorted(drought_fracs, key=drought_fracs.get, reverse=True)
    # Take every (1/val_ratio)-th tile from the top-drought end for val
    val_stems = set()
    step = max(1, len(sorted_stems) // val_n)
    for i in range(0, len(sorted_stems), step):
        if len(val_stems) >= val_n:
            break
        val_stems.add(sorted_stems[i])
    # Fallback: fill val_n if not reached
    for s in sorted_stems:
        if len(val_stems) >= val_n: break
        val_stems.add(s)

    print(f'Clean masks total: {len(all_clean_masks)}, val: {len(val_stems)}')
    print(f'Val tile drought fracs: {[round(drought_fracs[s],3) for s in val_stems]}')

    # Build sub-datasets
    # CleanMaskDataset filters by directory listing — for the train/val split
    # we create two filtered subfolder views using symlinks-or-list
    # Simpler: instantiate one ClanMaskDataset and split via Subset
    full_clean = CleanMaskDataset(
        images_dir=clean_images_dir,
        masks_dir=clean_masks_dir,
        crop_size=crop_size,
        train=True,
    )
    train_idx, val_idx = [], []
    for i, (img, mask) in enumerate(full_clean.pairs):
        if mask.stem in val_stems:
            val_idx.append(i)
        else:
            train_idx.append(i)
    print(f'  Clean train: {len(train_idx)}, val: {len(val_idx)}')

    from torch.utils.data import Subset
    clean_train = Subset(full_clean, train_idx) if train_idx else None

    # Val-mode clean (no augmentation, centre crop)
    full_clean_val = CleanMaskDataset(
        images_dir=clean_images_dir,
        masks_dir=clean_masks_dir,
        crop_size=crop_size,
        train=False,
    )
    val_subset = Subset(full_clean_val, val_idx) if val_idx else None

    pseudo_train = PseudoLabelDataset(
        images_dir=paths['unlabeled_dir'],
        masks_dir=Path(paths.get('pseudo_masks_dir', '/content/v5_data/pseudo_masks')),
        crop_size=crop_size,
        train=True,
    )

    composite_train = CompositeDataset(
        patches_dir=paths['patches_dir'],
        backgrounds_dir=paths['unlabeled_dir'],
        crop_size=crop_size,
        virtual_length=cfg_seg.get('composite_per_epoch', 1000),
        train=True,
        seed=cfg_seg.get('seed', 42),
    )

    mixed = MixedSegmentationDataset(
        clean=clean_train,
        pseudo=pseudo_train,
        composite=composite_train,
        ratio_clean    = cfg_seg.get('batch_manual',    4),
        ratio_pseudo   = cfg_seg.get('batch_pseudo',    4),
        ratio_composite= cfg_seg.get('batch_composites', 8),
    )
    print(f'  Mixed train length: {len(mixed)}')
    print(f'  Composite per epoch: {cfg_seg.get("composite_per_epoch", 1000)}')

    dl_train = DataLoader(
        mixed,
        batch_size=cfg_seg['batch_size'],
        shuffle=True,
        num_workers=cfg_seg.get('num_workers', 4),
        pin_memory=True,
        drop_last=True,
        collate_fn=collate_fn,
    )
    dl_val = (
        DataLoader(val_subset,
                   batch_size=cfg_seg['batch_size'],
                   shuffle=False, num_workers=2,
                   pin_memory=True, collate_fn=collate_fn)
        if val_subset is not None else None
    )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Class pixel counts (from training clean masks, for weighting)
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    print('\nComputing class pixel counts from clean training masks...')
    class_counts = {i: 0 for i in range(NUM_CLASSES)}
    for i in train_idx:
        _, mask_path = full_clean.pairs[i]
        m = cv2.imread(str(mask_path), cv2.IMREAD_GRAYSCALE)
        for c in range(NUM_CLASSES):
            class_counts[c] += int((m == c).sum())
    total = max(sum(class_counts.values()), 1)
    print('  Pixel distribution (training clean):')
    for c in range(NUM_CLASSES):
        pct = class_counts[c] / total * 100
        print(f'    {CLASS_NAMES[c]:14} {pct:5.2f}%')

    drought_idx    = CLASS_TO_IDX.get('drought', 6)
    cotton_idx     = CLASS_TO_IDX.get('cotton_ball', 3)

    # Drought weight override: force high weight so model must learn drought.
    drought_override = cfg_seg.get('drought_override_weight', 30.0)

    # Cotton_ball weight override: auto-computed weight (~32) was too high with
    # 151 clean tiles, causing cotton_ball false positives on bright soil pixels
    # (Balaji v4 showed 15.3% cotton vs 1.1% in v3). Lowering to 12 reduces
    # sensitivity while still giving adequate supervision for the rare class.
    cotton_override = cfg_seg.get('cotton_override_weight', None)

    overrides = {drought_idx: drought_override}
    if cotton_override is not None:
        overrides[cotton_idx] = float(cotton_override)

    class_weights = make_class_weights(
        class_counts, NUM_CLASSES,
        overrides=overrides,
    )
    print('  Computed class weights (drought + cotton overridden):')
    for ci, w in enumerate(class_weights.tolist()):
        print(f'    {CLASS_NAMES[ci]:14} {w:6.2f}')

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Model
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    print('\nBuilding SegFormer-B2 ...')
    model = SegFormerB2(
        num_classes=NUM_CLASSES,
        input_size=crop_size,
        pretrained_id=cfg_seg.get('pretrained_id', 'nvidia/mit-b2'),
        pretrained_local_path=cfg_seg.get('pretrained_local_path'),
        in_channels=9,
    ).to(device)
    n_params = sum(p.numel() for p in model.parameters()) / 1e6
    print(f'  parameters: {n_params:.1f}M')

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Loss + optimizer
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    criterion = SegmentationLoss(
        class_weights=class_weights.to(device),
        w_ce=cfg_seg.get('ce_weight', 1.0),
        w_dice=cfg_seg.get('dice_weight', 2.5),
        w_lovasz=cfg_seg.get('boundary_weight', 1.0),
        ignore_index=IGNORE_INDEX,
        focal_gamma=cfg_seg.get('focal_gamma', 2.0),
    ).to(device)

    optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW(
        model.parameters(),
        lr=cfg_seg['lr'],
        weight_decay=cfg_seg['weight_decay'],
    )
    scheduler = torch.optim.lr_scheduler.CosineAnnealingLR(
        optimizer, T_max=cfg_seg['epochs'], eta_min=1e-6
    )

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    # Training loop
    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    best_miou   = 0.0
    run_name    = cfg_seg.get('run_name', 'best_v2')
    best_path   = out_dir / f'{run_name}.pt'
    history     = []
    start_epoch = 1

    # Resume from checkpoint if requested
    if resume_path is not None and Path(resume_path).exists():
        print(f'\nResuming from {resume_path} ...')
        ckpt = torch.load(resume_path, map_location=device, weights_only=False)
        model.load_state_dict(ckpt['model_state_dict'])
        best_miou   = 0.0
        start_epoch = 1
        history     = []
        print('  V4 weights loaded; starting V5 from epoch 1.')
        print(f'  Resumed at epoch {start_epoch}, best mIoU so far: {best_miou:.4f}')
    elif resume_path is not None:
        print(f'[WARNING] --resume path not found: {resume_path}. Starting fresh.')


    if resume_path is None or not Path(resume_path).exists():
        raise FileNotFoundError("Provide an existing checkpoint with --resume")

    model.eval()
    t0 = time.time()

    biases = [round(0.9 + i * 0.05, 2) for i in range(13)]
    matrices = {
        b: np.zeros((NUM_CLASSES, NUM_CLASSES), dtype=np.int64)
        for b in biases
    }

    model.eval()
    with torch.inference_mode():
        for batch in tqdm(dl_val, desc="V11 calibration"):
            images = batch["image"].to(device)
            masks = batch["mask"].to(device)
            logits = model(images)

            for bias in biases:
                adjusted = logits.clone()
                adjusted[:, 6] -= bias
                preds = adjusted.argmax(dim=1)
                update_confusion(matrices[bias], preds, masks, NUM_CLASSES)

    print("\\nDrought bias calibration results")
    print(f"{'Bias':>7} {'mIoU':>10} {'Drought IoU':>14}")

    results = []
    for bias in biases:
        ious, miou = compute_iou_per_class(matrices[bias])
        results.append((bias, miou, ious[6]))
        print(f"{bias:7.2f} {miou*100:9.3f}% {ious[6]*100:13.3f}%")


    print("\nPer-class IoU at drought bias 1.20:")
    ious, _ = compute_iou_per_class(matrices[1.20])
    for name, iou in zip(CLASS_NAMES, ious):
        print(f"  {name:14}: {iou*100:.3f}%")

    best = max(results, key=lambda x: x[1])
    print(f"\\nBest tested bias: {best[0]:.2f}")
    print(f"Best validation mIoU: {best[1]*100:.4f}%")
    print(f"Baseline mIoU: {results[0][1]*100:.4f}%")
    return


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Entry point
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def main() -> None:
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument('--config', type=Path, default=Path('configs/config.yaml'))
    ap.add_argument('--resume', type=Path, default=None,
                    help='Resume from a previous checkpoint .pt file')
    args = ap.parse_args()

    import yaml
    with open(args.config) as f:
        config = yaml.safe_load(f)

    train(config, resume_path=args.resume)


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()




================================================================================
SOURCE FILE: src/segmentation/eval_confusion_v11.py
================================================================================

"""
SegFormer-B2 training loop.

Usage:
    python -m src.segmentation.train --config configs/config.yaml

What it does:
    1. Builds mixed dataset (clean + pseudo + synthetic).
    2. Loads SegFormer-B2 with 9-channel input adapter.
    3. Computes per-class loss weights from training pixel counts.
    4. Trains with CE + Dice + Lovász, with per-sample loss weights.
    5. Validates each epoch on held-out clean tiles.
    6. Saves best mIoU checkpoint, writes training_report.json.
    7. Dumps val_predictions/ with PNG overlays of best-epoch predictions.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import time
from pathlib import Path

import cv2
import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn as nn
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader
from torch.utils.tensorboard import SummaryWriter
from tqdm import tqdm

from src.preprocessing.tensor_builder import build_9channel
from src.segmentation.dataset import (
    CleanMaskDataset,
    CompositeDataset,
    MixedSegmentationDataset,
    PseudoLabelDataset,
    _augment_image_and_mask,
)
from src.segmentation.losses import SegmentationLoss, make_class_weights
from src.segmentation.model import SegFormerB2
from src.utils.constants import (
    CLASS_NAMES,
    CLASS_TO_IDX,
    IGNORE_INDEX,
    NUM_CLASSES,
    PALETTE,
)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def collate_fn(batch: list[dict]) -> dict:
    """Stack the dicts from PatchDataset → batched dict."""
    images       = torch.stack([b['image'] for b in batch])
    masks        = torch.stack([b['mask']  for b in batch])
    weights      = torch.tensor([b['loss_weight'] for b in batch],
                                dtype=torch.float32)
    sources      = [b['source'] for b in batch]
    return {
        'image':         images,
        'mask':          masks,
        'sample_weight': weights,
        'source':        sources,
    }


def compute_iou_per_class(
    confusion: np.ndarray,
) -> tuple[np.ndarray, float]:
    """
    Per-class IoU from a (num_classes, num_classes) confusion matrix
    where confusion[true, pred] = pixel count.
    """
    iou_per = np.zeros(NUM_CLASSES, dtype=np.float64)
    for c in range(NUM_CLASSES):
        tp = confusion[c, c]
        fp = confusion[:, c].sum() - tp
        fn = confusion[c, :].sum() - tp
        denom = tp + fp + fn
        iou_per[c] = tp / denom if denom > 0 else np.nan
    miou = np.nanmean(iou_per)
    return iou_per, miou


def update_confusion(
    confusion: np.ndarray,
    preds: torch.Tensor,
    targets: torch.Tensor,
    num_classes: int,
) -> None:
    """Add to confusion matrix in-place. preds/targets are torch (B, H, W)."""
    valid = (targets != IGNORE_INDEX)
    p = preds[valid].cpu().numpy().astype(np.int32)
    t = targets[valid].cpu().numpy().astype(np.int32)
    # bincount approach is fast
    idx = t * num_classes + p
    counts = np.bincount(idx, minlength=num_classes * num_classes)
    confusion += counts.reshape(num_classes, num_classes)


def save_prediction_overlay(
    img_rgb: np.ndarray,
    true_mask: np.ndarray,
    pred_mask: np.ndarray,
    save_path: Path,
) -> None:
    """Save a 3-panel comparison: original | true mask | predicted mask."""
    H, W = img_rgb.shape[:2]

    def colorize(m):
        out = np.zeros((*m.shape, 3), dtype=np.uint8)
        for i, c in enumerate(PALETTE):
            out[m == i] = c
        out[m == IGNORE_INDEX] = (90, 90, 90)
        return out

    true_color = colorize(true_mask)
    pred_color = colorize(pred_mask)

    # Side-by-side
    composite = np.hstack([img_rgb, true_color, pred_color])

    # Label bar at top
    bar = np.full((30, composite.shape[1], 3), 230, dtype=np.uint8)
    font = cv2.FONT_HERSHEY_SIMPLEX
    cv2.putText(bar, 'Original', (10, 22), font, 0.55, (20,20,20), 1, cv2.LINE_AA)
